# Gaps register: everything the protocol and the network do not cover yet

Started 2026-10-09. One place to track what is **not** covered, so we can come back to it. Every row says what protects us today, what would close the gap, and where the detail lives. When a gap is closed, change its status and add the spec-delta number; never delete a row.

**Priority:** A = needed before outsiders join the live beta; B = needed for the first provider pilot; C = later. **Status:** open, designed (a design exists), partial (some of it is built), closed.

## Partial: built in part, with work left

49 rows are partial (status column). This table is a view of them; the row in its section has the detail. Keep it in step when a row changes status (rule X3).

## 1. Agent drift and quality

| ID | Gap | Covered today by | Fix | Detail | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | No canary or regression suite; the gate is opt-in, not automatic | Lineage edges say what changed; the Mandate bounds actions | A small fixed task set with checks, run on every memory update and runtime swap; pass/fail recorded | backlog "Drift and failure-mode testing" (S62: `asp canary` and canary/default-suite.json built. **Correction, 2026-11-12: this row understated what S63 already built** — `asp verify`'s canary check is not empty; it reports, per package, which recorded memory, backend, harness or adapter changes cite a canary result, skip when none do (`packages/asp-package/src/package.ts` §6, docstring corrected). **Left:** the gate itself (`asp canary setup`, `canaryGateForChange`) runs the suite on a memory update or runtime swap only for an agent that has been set up with a canary target; nothing runs it automatically for an agent that has not, so "every" update is not yet covered, only the configured ones) | A | partial |
| D2 | No behavioural baseline or before/after comparison | Reputation, strikes, outcomes are in the log | Outcome series per agent and per principal, compared across each lineage edge | `docs/continual-learning-loop.md` §7 (S62: reports can be saved as baselines and compared, flagging regression and drift; no per-principal outcome series from the log yet) | A | partial |
| D3 | Memory loss is not detected | Memory hashes show that memory changed | Recall questions about earlier-known facts after each merge or prune | backlog | B | open |
| D4 | Which model weights actually ran is not recorded; a name can point to different weights | Runtime swap is a lineage edge | Record model name, provider and digest in the passport or Action; a change opens probation **Done so far (S66): the model name and provider are recorded in each Action's metrics. Left: no digest, and a model change does not open a probation.** **Closed 2026-11-12 (S182, lead):** the gateway records what the provider says ran (the resolved model, and the backend fingerprint as the digest where the provider gives one, for streamed and non-streamed replies); a later Action naming a new model, or the same name on a different digest, opens a 7-day probation (tests: gateway.test.ts, action.test.ts). A provider that gives no fingerprint (Anthropic) has the name only; the digest of a local open-weight model is split into DK96. | backlog "open models" (8) | A | closed |
| D5 | Probation is recorded but changes no rule | Shown in the lineage | Make it raise the risk floor or require a canary pass | backlog | B | open |
| D6 | No detection of prompt injection or goal hijacking | Mandate and gateway bound the actions that can follow | Injection tests in the canary; flag tool results that carry instructions | backlog (S62: the injection-in-file canary task measures whether a hidden instruction makes a model try a forbidden call; no detection of it) | A | partial |
| D7 | No reasoning-trace monitoring | none | Local-only analysis of visible reasoning as an earlier-firing signal | backlog "reasoning traces" | C | open |
| D8 | Quality, cost and time trends are not tracked | Actions carry a summary string | A structured `metrics` field (tokens, requests, seconds) on the Action, and a trend view **Done so far (S66): Actions carry structured metrics (tokens, requests, tool calls, seconds). Closed 2026-11-11: `asp trend` is the trend view (days and agents: sessions, calls, refusals, failures, tokens, time), counted from the machine's records and tested in `packages/asp-cli/test/today.test.ts`. It shows how often an agent hit a wall or failed, not whether its work was good (that is D2 and the canary).** | learning-loop doc | A | closed |
| D9 | Lessons are not verified before they enter memory | Memory budget, merge, undo by lineage | Reflection proposes, a gate checks evidence, conflicts and an A/B replay | learning-loop doc §3 | A | designed |
| D10 | The principal is not shown what was learned | Lineage records each memory edit | `asp learned`, then the mail section and dashboard with Keep, Edit, Undo. **Done so far (S157, 2026-11-11):** `asp learned` lists every memory change in an agent's package with what it added, changed and removed; `show` prints the lines; `keep` notes that the principal read it (a note on this machine, not signed); `undo` puts the files back as a new signed update that names the one it rolls back (`reverts`), refusing when the files changed again unless `--force`; `edit <file> --from|--text` replaces one memory file with the principal's text, also a signed update. Each memory change now keeps its before and after text in the package's `learned/` folder (tests in `packages/asp-cli/test/learned.test.ts`). **Done so far (S176, 2026-11-12):** `asp mail preview|queue|pending|watch --package <pkg>` reads the agent's own package locally and the end-of-job mail's "what changed in its memory" section now names what each change added, changed and removed and whether the principal already reviewed it (kept, edited, taken back, or not yet), matched to the log's memory entries by timestamp and description, with a line pointing at `asp learned` to act on it; without `--package` the mail keeps the plain lineage description, as before (tests in `packages/asp-package/test/mail-learned.test.ts`, `packages/asp-cli/test/mail.test.ts`). **Left:** the dashboard section with Keep, Edit and Undo (the dashboard is local-only, U2); "never remember this" marks; changes made before S157 have no text kept, so they can be kept but not undone | learning-loop doc §4 | A | partial |
| D11 | Shared lessons are not offered to runs automatically, and reviews are one-agent-one-vote | Commons search through the gateway's MCP tools | Offer reviewed lessons at run start; weight reviews by reputation; stake or slash bad entries | backlog | B | partial |
| D12 | No per-principal memory with a privacy boundary | none | A memory scope private to a principal-agent pair | learning-loop doc | A | open |
| D13 | Saved states: no snapshot, pause or rollback mid-run | Between-run lineage only | Save memory and environment at junctures | backlog | C | open |
| D14 | No active calibration. Nothing tunes an agent's beliefs or world model on real telemetry, and nothing measures value drift across runs against the values it was given (slide 9 card 3 is a design, not a result) | Memory write-back and penalty notes shape the next run; reports can be saved as baselines and compared (D2) | A calibration step that takes verified telemetry (D9), updates what the agent holds as lessons, and a drift measure over a lineage with a held-out check; decide first what "value drift" is measured against | deck slide 9; `docs/continual-learning-loop.md` | C | open |
| D15 | No audit of sub-agent telemetry before synthesis: a parent merges what its sub-agents report without a measured check of that telemetry (D9 covers lessons entering memory, not the telemetry a parent trusts mid-run) | Signed Actions per node; the court and canary check after the fact | A gate that samples or replays sub-agent Actions against their Mandates before the parent's result is accepted; a recorded pass or fail | deck slide 9 (State Verification, earlier wording); `docs/protocol-accountability.md` | B | open |
| D16 | The loop says "the next run starts from verified history" but only signed history is reloaded: the Invariant Audit Gate that would verify it is not built (D9), so the verified claim holds only once D9 closes. Telemetry Ingestion logs actions and refusals, not beliefs (action-conditioned belief logging is not captured, D7) | Signed log; memory write-back and reload for Claude Code (P5) | Close D9, then reword the loop label on slide 9 from signed to verified; decide whether belief logging needs reasoning traces (D7) | deck slide 9 | C | open |
| D17 | No utility tracing: nothing ties what a run spent (tokens, requests, seconds) to what it delivered, so "utility" cannot be traced per run or compared across runs; slide 6's Evals card names it and it is the least built part of that card | Actions carry structured metrics (D8, partly); the signed audit trail of every run; the evaluation harness | A per-run record of cost against accepted outcome; a view that compares a run with an agent's own baseline (D2); no use of the term "action-conditioned" until a measure exists | deck slide 6 card 5; D2, D8 | C | open |

## 2. Harm the Mandate does not name

| ID | Gap | Covered today by | Fix | Detail | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | A granted network scope can be used to hurt third parties (brute force, scanning, flooding); a swarm multiplies it | Contagion watcher (same input), strikes, kill switch S89: `network.rate` in the Mandate limits network calls a minute to any one host and in all; the gateway and the Claude Code and Antigravity pre-call hooks refuse a call over it, without a strike, and count it in the Action's metrics | Detectors for brute-force signatures (many failed-auth responses, repeated 401 and 403 from one host, port-scan patterns); fleet-wide caps so a swarm shares one budget instead of multiplying it; a rate by default for tier 1 and 2 (H15). **Done so far (S67, S68, S89): named hosts, and rate limits per host and in total.** | backlog "Harm that no Mandate names"  **Done so far (S67, S68, S89, S147, S200): named hosts, rate limits per host and in total, one shared count for copies on a machine, and detectors: a command that guesses credentials (a password list, a login loop) or probes ports in a loop is refused with a strike; through the egress proxy, ten 401 or 403 answers from one host in a minute (plain HTTP) or more than eight different ports of one host in a minute shuts the host for the run (tests: abuse.test.ts). Left: the failed-auth detector cannot read the answers of an HTTPS tunnel (H27); a person's agents on other machines share no budget (H24). The Claude Code and Antigravity pre-call hooks now carry the brute-force and port-scan command detector too, from the same source (H29, closed, S240).** | A | partial |
| H2 | No default-deny network for new or low-tier agents | Tier limits on spend and parallel nodes only | Egress allowed by tier and by named hosts **Done so far (S67): a tier 1 or 2 Mandate must name hosts and the gateway refuses others. Left: see H11 to H14.** | same **Closed 2026-11-12 (S200): egress is allowed by tier and by named hosts at every layer: a tier 1 or 2 Mandate with a network scope must name hosts and a rate (log rule, asp-log action.test.ts), the gateway refuses any other host and any host it cannot read (gateway.test.ts, egress.test.ts), and a sandbox never keeps the host's own network at any tier: no network scope means the gateway only, a network scope means the gateway as an egress proxy, also when the Mandate names no hosts (`sandboxNetwork`, abuse.test.ts). What was left is tracked in its own rows: H11, H12, H13 and H14.** | A | partial | closed 
| H3 | Third parties have no way to report | Reports need a passport and a deposit | Signed abuse report verified by proof of domain control | same | A | open |
| H4 | Bond covers the principal, not third parties Waits on the owner (docs/owner-decisions.md).| none | Bond sizing for network scopes; insurance pool | same | A | open |
| H5 | No outcome detectors (a secret was obtained or leaked) | Known-bad list needs a prior report | Detect secret-shaped output and credential use | same | B | open |
| H6 | Known-bad list is exact-match, shell commands only, has no expiry | Fingerprint of normalized command | Pattern-level matching; expiry and removal; other tool types | S54 | B | open |
| H7 | Tool calls the model writes as text, and code-writing agents, are invisible to the gateway | Hooks on the runtimes that have them | Text-format parsers; sandbox with egress rules | gateway doc §4 | B | open |
| H8 | No sandbox level: what a tool does inside the machine is not bounded | Assurance level exists in the schema | Container or OS sandbox wrapper | gateway doc P4 (S60: Linux bubblewrap sandbox built; Docker backend for Windows and macOS added in S61, live end to end in WSL, see the checklist) | B | partial |
| H10 | Actions an agent's own code takes outside the model loop are invisible to the gateway. Seen live: Aider saw a URL in the prompt, offered to scrape it, auto-installed Playwright and tried to download Chromium, then navigated to the URL, none of it through a tool call | The Mandate and gateway only see model-requested tool calls | Sandbox with egress rules (H8), network-level redirect through the gateway, and a list of known self-acting agents with their risky defaults | S59, live checklist (S60: with --sandbox the agent has no network unless the Mandate grants one; not yet re-run against Aider) **Closed 2026-11-12 (S200): the sandbox's only way out is the gateway's egress proxy (S92; egress.test.ts), so what an agent's own code does is bounded by the Mandate's hosts, its rate and the abuse detectors; a list of known self-acting agents (Aider, OpenHands, Goose, Codex, Claude Code, Antigravity) with their risky defaults and what to switch off is printed by `asp run` for a run that is not in a sandbox (`self-acting.ts`; abuse.test.ts). Re-running Aider live is H25.** | A | partial | closed 
| H11 | The host limit is enforced by the gateway and, since S68, by the Claude Code and Antigravity pre-call hooks. Codex and OpenHands runs only observe calls, so a host outside the list is not prevented there; page-bound browser actions (click, execute JavaScript) name no host | Gateway and two hooks | Run Codex and OpenHands through the gateway (or a sandbox with host egress, H13) | S67, S68 | B | partial |
| H12 | Hosts are read from the call's text: URLs and bare hosts after known network commands. A script that builds its URL (python -c, a downloaded program, an agent's own code) is refused if it looks like a network command with no readable host, but an ordinary command that fetches inside a program is not seen | Calls with an unreadable host are refused | The sandbox with egress filtering by host (H8, H10) | S67 | A | open |
| H13 | The sandbox's network was all or nothing: a Mandate that names hosts or a rate now leaves the sandbox with no network of its own and the gateway is an egress proxy (S92), live-checked in bubblewrap (WSL). What is left: the Docker backend was not run live; one request is counted per HTTPS tunnel however many it carries; only HTTP and HTTPS (a raw TCP tool such as ssh has no route, so it fails closed, but cannot be allowed by host either); a Mandate with a network scope and no hosts or rate (tier 3 and up) still gets the open network | The host list (S67), the rate limit (S89), the egress proxy (S92) | Run the Docker backend live; per-request counting inside tunnels needs TLS interception, which is not planned; allow named raw-TCP hosts | Run the Docker backend live (H26); per-request counting inside tunnels needs TLS interception, which is not planned (H27); allow named raw-TCP hosts (H28) **Done so far (S67, S92, S200): a sandbox with a network scope and no hosts or rate (tier 3 and up) no longer gets the host's open network; it gets the egress proxy too (private addresses refused, scans and failed logins watched).** | A | partial |
| H14 | MCP tools that reach the network (a `mcp.*` scope) are not host-checked, and the Mandate has no per-host or per-minute rate limit | none | Rate limits per host and in total; host arguments for known MCP tools **Closed 2026-11-14 (S340).** An MCP tool that names a destination in its arguments (url, uri, href, link, address, endpoint, base_url, host, hostname, domain, origin, target, server, proxy, or any value that is a URL, nested up to three levels) is now host-checked against the Mandate's `network.hosts` and counted against the per-host and total rate, by the gateway's judge and by both runtimes' pre-call hooks (Claude Code and Antigravity), whose copies agree. A host outside the list is refused with the reason and no strike; a URL in one key does not hide another in a second key; with no hosts in the Mandate nothing changes. Test: `packages/asp-package/test/mcp-hosts.test.ts` (refusal and allowance by host, wildcard, nested and bare destinations, the second URL, the no-hosts and no-destination cases, the host read for the rate, the gateway limiter and both hooks' ledgers refuse the fourth call a minute to one host, and the hooks' readers equal the gateway's). **Left, split (DK240):** an MCP server configured with a fixed endpoint, so that no destination is in the call, cannot be host-checked at the call: only the sandbox's egress proxy (H13) reaches it, and that is a live run. | S67 | A | closed |
| DK240 | An MCP tool whose destination is not in its arguments (a server configured to a fixed endpoint, or one that resolves its target itself) is not host-checked at the call: it is counted against the per-server and total rate, but the host it reaches is unknown, so an agent with `mcp.*` scopes and hosts in its Mandate can reach a host the Mandate does not name through such a tool. | The host check and rate of H14, the egress proxy of H13 | Either the Mandate names the MCP servers an agent may call and the sandbox's egress proxy enforces the hosts for the process that runs them, or an MCP server is refused unless its destination is declared. Done: a test in which a sandboxed agent calls a fixed-endpoint MCP tool that reaches a host outside the Mandate and the egress proxy refuses it, run live with a real MCP server. Needs the sandbox and a live run. Left from H14. | Split from H14 2026-11-14; H13, H8, H11; size M | A | open |
| H15 | Tier 1 and 2 now must carry a rate (log rule, S92) and every Mandate with a network scope gets a default from `asp market mandate`; what is left: a swarm of copies each gets its own allowance (n copies make n times the calls), the rate is per agent not per principal or per tenant, and MCP network tools (H14) and runs outside the gateway and the two hooks (Codex and OpenHands without the gateway) are not limited | The host list (S67), the contagion watcher, strikes, the log rule and default rate (S92), the egress proxy (S92) | A budget shared across a fleet's copies (per principal and per tenant); the same limit for MCP tools **Done so far (S147): gateways on one machine that run under the same Mandate share one rate count in a locked file (n copies no longer make n times the calls), `--fleet-rate` (or `ASP_FLEET_RATE`) caps everything one person runs on the machine, and a call to an MCP tool counts as one request to its server. Left: H24 (another machine; the pre-call hooks).** | S89, S92, S147 | A | partial |
| H16 | The hooks counted calls in a file without a lock, so a burst of parallel calls could pass a limit. Now locked (S92) and tested with 14 processes at once for both hooks; the window is still one minute only, which is H18 | The ledger lock (S92) | none | S89, S92 | C | closed |
| H17 | The rate limit counted tool calls, not requests, so one shell command with many requests (a loop around curl, fifty URLs) passed as one call. Now counted as the requests it makes (S90) and live-checked with real Claude Code (a command of eight curls under a limit of three: refused whole, the site saw none). What is left: it is a reading of the command text, so a script that makes its own requests (python, node, a binary) counts as one, a loop whose bound is computed counts as 1000 (refused, even if it is small), and a command built at run time is not seen | The gateway and hooks read the text; the sandbox without network (H13) stops everything | Enforce in the network layer (the sandbox's egress, H13); or run scripts under a request counter | Enforce in the network layer (the sandbox's egress, H13); or run scripts under a request counter **Done so far (S90, S200): a project script named in a command (python, node, a shell script) is read: a request in a loop counts as 1000, a `range(N)` as N, the hosts are the URLs in the file, and a `shell.exec` call that runs one is counted when the Mandate grants shell.network (script-requests.ts; script-requests.test.ts). Left: a script that imports an installed package, a binary, or a target built at run time; counting what a script really connects to, inside HTTPS tunnels (H27). S240: the script reader now follows one level of a script's own relative imports, and is carried into the Claude Code and Antigravity pre-call hooks too (H29, closed), so a shell.exec call that runs a project script is rate-counted there the same way the gateway counts it.** | A | partial |
| H18 | The rate limit has one window, a minute: no per-second burst limit and no hourly or daily cap, so an agent can spend a whole day's allowance at the maximum rate, and a slow scan (one request every two seconds) is never limited | The per-minute limit (S89) | More windows in `network.rate` (a schema change); a daily budget per agent | S92 | B | open |
| H19 | No Sybil admission gate at the entry of a swarm: nothing requires hardware attestation or a staking threshold before an agent joins a cohort, so many cheap identities can be created and run together (slide 10 block 1) | Tier gating, bonds below tier 3 with named hosts only, one starter grant per Google account (M9), juror exclusion by sponsor (BM11) | Hardware or platform attestation of the runtime for admission; a stake threshold that scales with the size of the cohort; admission limits per sponsor; a test that many fronts cannot join as one cohort | deck slide 10; M9, BM11 | B | open |
| H20 | The behavioural watcher sees one thing: the same dangerous input spreading across jobs, and only with 3 or more agents (BM10). It does not watch policy drift across a cohort or correlated anomalies that are not the same command (slide 10 block 3) | Contagion watcher on the same normalized input, reports with a deposit | Correlated-anomaly signals across nodes (same target, same timing, same tool sequence); a drift signal from the canary (D1, D2); a lower threshold with a false-positive measure | deck slide 10; BM10, H1 | B | open |
| H21 | Cohort quarantine is not automatic or low-latency: a freeze needs a report, a panel and an upheld ruling, so containment waits for a person or the operator (slide 10 block 4) | Cohort stop on an upheld report, with `--spare` for jobs without a ruling; the kill switch for one job | An automatic hold on the cohort the moment the watcher fires, reversed by the panel if wrong; a measured time from first harm to freeze; the report cap (BM10) raised or removed | deck slide 10; BM10, M10 | B | open |

| H23 | Telemetry at the scale of millions of agent streams is not built or measured: the watcher and the log were tested with a dozen agents, not with a low-latency layer at scale (slide 10 card Dynamic Parallelism) | The evaluation harness with a mixed swarm of scripted and real agents | A streaming layer with sampling; a load test with a stated size and latency target; a decision on what must stay in the signed log and what can be sampled | deck slide 10 | C | open |
| H24 | The shared rate count (H15) is a file on one machine: a person's agents on two machines, or a tenant's on many, each have their own allowance until the service counts them; the pre-call hooks (Antigravity, Claude Code under a contract) keep a count per run folder, so their copies are not shared and an MCP tool is not counted there; a refused call over the fleet limit is still counted against the Mandate's | The per-run counts, the Mandate's rate, the log service's record quota | A rate count held by the service per principal and tenant, the hooks pointed at the shared ledger and counting MCP tools, and a fleet limit that does not over-count | S147 | B | open |

| H27 | A sandbox's HTTPS tunnel is opaque: the proxy counts one request per tunnel however many it carries, cannot count requests inside it, and cannot see 401 or 403 answers, so the failed-auth detector (H1) and per-request counting (H17) see plain HTTP only | The per-tunnel count, the host and port checks, the command text readers, the script reader | Per-request counting and answer reading inside tunnels need TLS interception with a certificate the sandbox trusts; not planned: needs the owner's decision (it weakens the agent's end-to-end TLS, and the person must trust a local authority) | S92, S200 | B | open |

## 3. Portability and runtimes

| ID | Gap | Covered today by | Fix | Detail | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | Only four runtimes have adapters (Claude Code, Codex, Antigravity, OpenHands) | The gateway reaches any agent that can set a base URL or an MCP server | Top 20 runtimes through the gateway, then deeper adapters for the few that matter. **Designed 2026-10-15: docs/portability-at-scale.md (a declarative runtime descriptor and a generic adapter, MCP tools with annotation defaults, a generated support matrix)** | backlog "Portability", `docs/gateway-design.md` | A | partial |
| P2 | Gemini API and non-OpenAI-compatible local APIs are not judged | OpenAI chat, Responses and Anthropic are | Gemini adapter in the gateway; Ollama native | backlog | B | open |
| P3 | Codex under a ChatGPT login cannot be pointed at the gateway | Codex with an API key or a custom provider can | An API-key route, or a network-level redirect | S58 | B | open |
| P4 | Hosted and enterprise agents that cannot be wrapped | none | Self-report SDK (assurance level 1) | gateway doc P4 (S60: TypeScript and Python self-report SDKs built and checked across languages; no real vendor trial) | B | partial |
| P5 | Memory write-back is verified live only for Claude Code (and any MCP agent through the gateway) | Gateway memory tools | Check or add per runtime | backlog | B | partial |
| P6 | No model matrix: which open models call tools reliably | none | Run the swarm scenario across models | backlog (S62: first model matrix in docs/model-matrix.md (free-tier snapshot); no paid or Anthropic/OpenAI models, one agent loop) | B | partial |
| P7 | Weak models: malformed tool arguments, loops after a refusal | Strike limit ends loops | Measure and document per model | backlog (S62: the survives-a-refusal task measures looping after a refusal per model) | B | partial |
| P8 | No public support matrix and no per-runtime conformance test | none | Matrix: runtime by assurance level, with a test each | gateway doc P5 | A | open |
| P10 | OpenCode 1.18.35 starts but fails under the gateway with an unexplained server error (run config via `opencode.json` and `-m asp/<model>`); not yet working | Goose, Codex and Claude Code work through the gateway | Debug OpenCode's server mode and its provider config | S59 | B | open |
| P11 | Gemini CLI 0.63.0 installed but not checked: it talks the Gemini API (not judged) and needs a Gemini API key, not the subscription login | none | Gemini API support in the gateway and a key | S59 | B | open |
| P12 | Agents that edit through a text format (Aider) never use structured tool calls, so the gateway can observe but not enforce | Assurance is reported as `gateway_observed` for them | Text-format parsers or a sandbox; document per agent | S59 | B | open |
| P9 | Free-tier hosted models are not reproducible | none | Pin a paid or self-hosted model for the matrix | backlog | C | open |

## 4. Money, banks and Courts

| ID | Gap | Covered today by | Fix | Detail | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | The bank and credits are mock; no real money, rails or cash-out | Real rules and arithmetic on mock credits | A real custodian or payment partner signing the same Settlements | `docs/protocol-accountability.md` §8 | C | open |
| M2 | The beta's court is the operator's (S97): tenants cannot seat jurors, the operator seats three and rules with keys held on its own machine, and the service accepts no ruling while no panel can be drawn; what is left: one operator is a single point of trust and of delay, community jurors (the `verified` mode: a Google account, a minimum stake, one seat each) exist but are untested live and a Sybil defence beyond one seat for each Google account is not built, and the panel is drawn from seated jurors only so a few jurors means a small panel Waits on the owner (docs/owner-decisions.md).| Deterministic panel draw, stake, conflict exclusion, the court guard and operator tools (S97) | Community jurors with reputation, rotating and publicly named; juror pay and slashing for overturned rulings (M3) | S97 | A | partial |
| M3 | No appeals; no slashing of a juror for an overturned ruling | none | Appeal record and juror stake at risk | backlog | C | open |
| M4 | No delayed settlement or clawback for claims that need time to prove | Evidence grades on claims | Hold-back and clawback with a deadline | backlog (decision 2026-10-08: not yet) | C | open |
| M5 | Incentive constants (5% panel fee, 250‰ per slash, 20% reporter share) are untested against adversaries | Judgment calls | Simulation and a pilot | | B | open |
| M6 | A newcomer can now run a first job: newcomers are tier 1 (so they can bond), and a verified-Google tenant claims starter credits (S96: 500 each for up to two identities, once per Google account, from a capped pool) and a first job within tier 1's limits was tested end to end through the service. What is left: a newcomer has jobs to take only from people it knows (no discovery of jobs or agents, which is the society layer), a sponsor-backed first bond works only because the sponsor holds credits (starter credits go to the sponsor too), and the grant size and tier-1 limits are guesses to tune from the first live run | Starter credits and the pool cap (S96); tier 1 spend and parallel limits (S19) | Discovery of open jobs (the society layer); tuning from live data | S96 | B | partial |
| M7 | Compute is not priced into credits | Gateway meters tokens | Metering to credits | backlog | C | open |
| M8 | Subcontracting: only the basic nesting rules | S18 | Per-role Mandates inside one job | backlog | C | open |
| M9 | Starter credits are minted from nothing and tied only to a Google account each: someone with many Google accounts can claim many grants until the pool is spent (200000 credits, 200 grants by default), and credits mean nothing outside the service, but a pool spent on Sybil accounts leaves real newcomers with none; the grant is not tied to a parent or guardian's identity beyond the declaration | One grant per Google account (kept after closing), three tenants a Google account, the sign-up caps (S93, S94), a pool cap, the operator's suspend | A waiting list when the pool runs low; a grant that grows with a first settled job instead of being given up front; the operator topping up the pool on purpose | S96 **Closed 2026-11-11 (S160).** The grant is in two parts: 100 credits at once for each of up to two identities (enough for a first job: an escrow and a bond), and 400 more each when one of them has had a job accepted (released when the tenant looks at its account or asks again, drawn from the pool then, not reserved at the claim). When the pool cannot cover a claim, or others are waiting ahead, the tenant joins a waiting list and is told its place; `asp serve starter --tokens <file> [--add n | --starter-pool n]` shows the pool and its waiting list and changes its size on purpose, which the running service reads without a restart. One Google account fills at most 200 credits of pool before any job is accepted, not 1,000. Tests: `packages/asp-cli/test/starter.test.ts`. **The limit that stays (now a tracked row, DK69):** two accounts that accept each other's jobs can still earn the rest of the grant, so the pool's size, which the operator sets, is the cap on what abuse can cost, and credits are not money (the terms say so) | S102, S160 | A | closed |
| M10 | A dispute waits for the operator's ruling with no time limit: the operator is mailed when one opens (S98) but nothing rules by default after a deadline, so one slow answer leaves a principal's escrow locked | `asp court docket`, the operator's mail (S98) | A deadline after which the escrow splits by a stated rule **Closed 2026-10-28 (S151): a Contract may carry `ruling_window_seconds`; once a dispute has waited that long with no ruling the bank may settle with basis `ruling_timeout`, and the log requires the stated split (half the escrow released, nothing slashed, the whole bond returned, no fees): neither side is found at fault for the delay. A Contract with no window keeps the old behaviour (a dispute waits for its ruling). Mirrored in both SDKs (TypeScript and Python), the schemas, the lifecycle table, four new conformance vectors, the log's amount rule, and `asp market contract --ruling-window-seconds` and `asp market settle --basis ruling_timeout`; tests in `ruling-timeout.test.ts`. The split (half the escrow released, nothing slashed, the bond returned) was confirmed by the owner on 2026-10-28. The bank still has to issue the settlement: a bank that does not (the operator down) is the continuity problem of GV6.** | S97, S98, S151 | A | closed |

| P22 | Provider terms limit how the gateway may be used with a Claude subscription login. Anthropic's Legal and compliance page (read 2026-10-11) says OAuth login is for subscribers' ordinary use of Claude Code, that third-party developers may not route requests through Free, Pro or Max credentials on behalf of their users or collect, store or intermediate Claude.ai credentials, and that products should use API keys; it does not stop an end user signing in to the unmodified Claude Code binary with their own subscription, and its LLM-gateway page describes a gateway forwarding subscription traffic (with the `anthropic-beta` OAuth capability) as a supported setup. So the gateway is acceptable only as the user's own local process; it must never be hosted so that subscription credentials pass through our servers; hosted or always-on agents need the user's own API key (billed to them); and a society of agents running all day is not 'ordinary individual use' of a subscription | The gateway runs on the user's machine and forwards unchanged (it forwards `anthropic-beta`); the service never sees provider credentials | **Done so far (S108):** the rule is written (docs/provider-terms.md, terms section 6); headless runs already refuse without an API key. | T22 | A sub-agent's short-lived, revocable key can be avoided or left unbound: a node grant that cites no Mandate is limited only by the log's 24-hour cap (no `max_parallel`, no end at the Mandate's expiry); a node key bound to one Mandate is not checked against the contract it signs for; nothing requires that work done for a contract by a helper runs under a node, so a helper can be a separate long-lived agent identity; and a sub-agent inside a runtime (Claude Code or Codex parallel agents) is one process, one identity, with no key of its own | A node must expire (`node_ttl`), is capped at 24 hours, cannot outlive a Mandate it cites (`node_outlives_mandate`), cannot sign identity records or co-sign, stops signing at expiry (`node_key_expired`), and is revoked at once by chaining or when its holder's key is ruled suspect; `asp orchestrate` revokes an abandoned node | ~~Under a running contract a node grant must cite~~ (done, S137: a node key signs for a contract only if it was granted under that contract's own Mandate) ~~that contract's Mandate; a node key may sign only for the contract of its Mandate~~; also checked, no change needed: a separate identity cannot sign work on a contract (the lifecycle's role guards refuse any issuer but the performer, with a node signing as its person; a helper must be hired through a subcontract), and a Mandate with `nodes.max_parallel` 0 refuses every node grant citing it. Also done (S138): one public key cannot be registered twice under a DID (`node_key_reused`), and a node at the Mandate's limit can be revoked (it was refused before: found by a flood test). Not capped: grants under no Mandate (only the tenant's quota and rate limit apply) and a key reused across different DIDs. Also done (S139): an Action from a Claude Code run carries `sub_agents: shared_identity`, a label saying the runtime's own helpers share one identity (it gives them no keys; other runtimes are unmarked until we know whether they run helpers). **Done 2026-11-12:** `asp orchestrate --mandate <id>` is the safe part of DK-R-3 (round2-proposed-rows.md): a node's grant can now cite a real Mandate instead of leaving every node mandate-less and bounded only by the flat 24-hour cap. The Mandate must be issued to the orchestrating agent (checked once, before any node is spawned, not with `--isolate`, whose copies did not exist when the Mandate was issued); a node the Mandate refuses (`nodes.max_parallel` exceeded, past the Mandate's own expiry) fails that node outright instead of letting it run ungoverned, unlike a mandate-less grant, which stays best-effort bookkeeping (MOCKS.md) since nothing is at stake. `market mandate --node-max-parallel <n>` (default 1, was hardcoded to 1) makes citing one worth doing. Tests: `packages/asp-cli/test/orchestrate-mandate.test.ts`. Still to do: a live orchestrate run that tries each bypass; nothing yet requires that a contract's helper runs under a node at all (a subcontract can still hire a separate long-lived agent instead of `--mandate`); the 24-hour cap itself is unchanged (DK-R-3's other half, a decision for residency, is on the owner checklist) | docs/testing-map.md UC-249, UC-310, docs/live-run-identity.md | A | partial |
| T23 | The test "two sessions of one agent at once" (packages/asp-cli/test/run-concurrent.test.ts) is unreliable on Linux CI: it stopped with "stuck waiting for session D to finish" on the Ubuntu jobs in PR 11's first run (while a cleanup that was later taken out was in) and on Ubuntu Node 24 in one of PR 12's two runs, and passed in the other runs of the same code; the failing job ran about 13 minutes, so load is the likely cause, but the root cause is not found and a change to how approvals are read cannot be ruled out | The test has a hard 120-second timeout, labelled waits and a retrying approve; it passes on Windows and locally; a failed job is re-run | Find the cause (what session D is waiting for, and whether the standing job's approvals queue can stall under load); make the test report it; until then a red Ubuntu run on this test alone is re-run once and noted. **Found 2026-11-06 (PR 16):** it now fails on three of four node jobs plus postgres (Ubuntu 22.18 and 24, Windows 24), so it is no longer rare; it passes about 7 runs in 8 locally. Session D's request file is written the moment D starts, but D's "approval needed" line comes about 10 seconds later, behind session C's Checkpoint, which `asp stop` leaves open. D's Checkpoint is raised and resolved (job Running, 11 records), no answer file is left in any approvals folder, and the agent's request to the gateway times out after 300 s: so D's answer was either never written or was read and not acted on. Not yet known which. The test now has the service limits off, answers approvals until D finishes, and prints each session's output and the approval folders' files on failure. **Read from CI 2026-11-11 (the trail worked):** session D's request was seen at once but its Checkpoint was raised 11 s later, after session C (stopped, its Checkpoint left open) was answered; after that D's side logged nothing more, yet the job was Running with every Checkpoint answered: so an answer closed D's Checkpoint without being the answer D was waiting for, and D waited for one about its own Checkpoint, for ever. Cause found in `market resolve`: it answered "the latest Checkpoint in the chain" and `asp approvals` did not say which one the person had been shown, so an answer meant for an earlier request could close a later one. **Changed (unproven until CI is green five runs in a row):** `market resolve --about <id>` is refused unless that is the open Checkpoint; `asp approvals approve|refuse` passes the one it listed; a request whose Checkpoint was closed by an answer that was not about it is refused at once with a reason, not left waiting; the test prints each answer with its time, and the approval side prints state and the answers' targets while it waits (`ASP_DEBUG_APPROVALS=1`). Next: five green CI runs, then close **Root cause found 2026-11-12 (ci-fix worker, merged; docs/ci-findings.md):** every `asp run` made a tokenless probe to the forum, and the log service counted it as a failed sign-in, so after about ten sessions from one address the address was locked out and every later request waited out undici 300 s timeout. Fix: only a presented wrong token counts (`packages/asp-log/src/server.ts`). Locally the per-command approval test went from 310 s to 22 s and both approval tests pass 5 of 5 alone. **Not yet confirmed on CI** (the fix is unpushed): close this row after five consecutive green CI runs. **Closed 2026-11-12:** five consecutive green CI runs on claude/open-forum after the fix (44d8606, f5a6077, 9e0b4f4, 8e8f402, a719421), including the first Postgres runs of migrations 015 and 016. | docs/testing-map.md UC-138 | A | closed |

| T25 | The shell scanner read a PowerShell environment-variable assignment (`$env:NAME = value`) as "a verb built from a variable" (`shell.indirect`), the same false positive as T24. | Fixed in packages/asp-package/src/gateway/shell-scan.ts: the assignment pattern accepts a scope prefix (`$env:`); a literal on the right is data, a command on the right is still scanned (`$env:X = curl ...` is `shell.network`). Tests in packages/asp-package/test/shell-scan.test.ts. | Other PowerShell constructs may still be misread. | docs/live-run-checklist.md | B | closed |
| T26 | The bond floor v2 (S143) has no cutover rule: a log that ran under `legacy` and is then replayed under `v2` would refuse the low bonds it accepted before, so `verify()` and checkpoint replay would fail. Until a cutover exists v2 is for a new log or a test, and the hosted log stays on `legacy`. | Not fixed: the rule that decides from which record onward v2 applies (a number in the log's own configuration record, so replay is the same everywhere) is to be designed. | The positive terms use only accepted jobs; the Stage A5 to C evidence is about improvements, which are not in the log yet. | docs/credits-and-learning-experiment.md | A | designed |

The slide's closing line ("aggregating runtime tethers converts individual execution into a self-correcting alignment ecosystem", rewritten 2026-10-23) claims a result the study would have to show; nothing self-corrects yet | Strikes, slashes and tiers are recorded in the log; the swarm driver (T27) can produce long runs | Choose the one measure first (strikes per thousand runs by cohort age, with a baseline cohort); run it on the long swarm runs (T27); write the result either way; reword the slide's card to what was measured | deck slide 14 card 1; T27, D2 | C | open |

## 6. Operating the network

| ID | Gap | Covered today by | Fix | Detail | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| O1 | The log service had no TLS: tokens and records crossed the network in the clear unless a proxy was put in front | Behind a proxy | Done 2026-10-10 (S77): `asp serve --tls-cert --tls-key` serves HTTPS with HSTS; bound to a network address without TLS it refuses to start unless `--allow-plain-http` says a proxy terminates TLS. Tested over HTTPS in-process and end to end with a self-signed certificate. See O15 for certificate handling. | backlog | A | closed |
| O2 | The log service limits tenants and addresses, counts and caps what each writes, and an operator can suspend a tenant or block an address; a stranger can sign up on their own (S93) but with no verified identity, there is no limit sharing across processes, and no remote admin | S77 limits; S79: a quota on records and bytes per tenant (default 100000 records and 256 MB; admins none; counted at the service and kept in `<tokens>.usage.json`), `asp serve suspend|resume|block|unblock|usage` taking effect without a restart | A verified identity at sign-up (Google sign-in, U1; today the contact line is not checked, so a person can sign up repeatedly from different addresses, bounded by the proof of work and the day's cap); limits shared across several service processes and kept over a restart; tuning from real traffic. **Done so far (S77, S78, S79, S93: sign-up with proof of work, terms, per-address and daily caps, small starting quotas, an operator switch). Left: the rest of this cell, and O17, O18.** **Done so far (S150): with `asp serve --limits-state <file>` several service processes on one machine share three things in files under a lock, and they survive a restart: the lockout after failed sign-ins (addresses kept only as a salted hash), the per-tenant, per-write and per-address request buckets (each process publishes what it used about twice a second and takes the others' use out of its own bucket, so they act as one bucket to within half a second of traffic; a process that stops is forgotten after ten seconds), and the sign-up checks and the add are one locked step, so the day's cap, an address's limit and a name hold across processes (without the lock five processes signing up at once broke the cap every time in the test). The operator's suspend, resume and purge take the same lock, so they cannot write over a new sign-up. A count of people turned away because the day was full (a number a day, no details) shows in `asp serve signups`. Tests: `limits-state.test.ts`, `signup-shared.test.ts`. Left: nothing is shared between machines, which needs the database (a counter table in Postgres behind the same interface; not built, and it could not be tested without a Postgres here); a bucket first seen by a process starts full, so a new tenant can get up to one burst more per process; tuning the limits from real traffic (the turned-away count is the first number for it); a remote admin; a verified-contact tier.** | backlog, S150 | A | partial |
| O17 | The usage counts are the service's own tally of what it accepted, kept in a file beside the tokens file: they miss records already in the log when the service started, restart from nothing if the file is deleted, and are per process. The quota covers log records only; commons entries, known-bad posts and package uploads have their own or no limits | `<tokens>.usage.json`; package storage has its own quota | Count from the log itself (records by issuer's tenant); one quota across all the service's routes | S79 | B | open |
| O18 | Suspending and blocking are local commands that edit files next to the service: there is no remote admin interface, no record of who suspended whom or why beyond the reason text, and a blocked address behind a proxy is the proxy's unless `--trust-proxy` is set | The tokens file and block list, readable by the operator | An admin API with an audit trail; a signed record of a suspension | S79 | A | open |
| O19 | Sign-up with Google (S94) puts a verified Google account behind each tenant, but the command-line route (proof of work only) is unverified and is closed in production by --signup-require-google; the remaining costs to an abuser are a Google account each (cheap to make), three tenants a Google account, three a day per address and the day's cap; a botnet of many addresses can use the whole day's cap (200 by default) and lock real people out; there is no way for a tenant to get more quota except an operator editing the tokens file; no terms text exists yet (the operator supplies the address); the sign-up counts are per file so two service processes sharing one tokens file would each allow the cap | The proof of work, the caps, the quotas, suspend and block (S79, S93) | Google sign-in at sign-up (U1); a verified-contact tier with larger quotas; a waiting list when the cap is reached; the terms and privacy text (yours); counts shared across processes **Done so far (S150): with `asp serve --limits-state <file>` several service processes on one machine share three things in files under a lock, and they survive a restart: the lockout after failed sign-ins (addresses kept only as a salted hash), the per-tenant, per-write and per-address request buckets (each process publishes what it used about twice a second and takes the others' use out of its own bucket, so they act as one bucket to within half a second of traffic; a process that stops is forgotten after ten seconds), and the sign-up checks and the add are one locked step, so the day's cap, an address's limit and a name hold across processes (without the lock five processes signing up at once broke the cap every time in the test). The operator's suspend, resume and purge take the same lock, so they cannot write over a new sign-up. A count of people turned away because the day was full (a number a day, no details) shows in `asp serve signups`. Tests: `limits-state.test.ts`, `signup-shared.test.ts`. Left: a verified-contact tier with larger quotas (it needs the service to send a code by email); a waiting list that keeps contact details (it needs the privacy text, which is yours); the terms and privacy text (yours); how many Google accounts a determined person can make is not something the service can limit.** | S93, S150 | A | partial |
| O15 | Certificates are the operator's problem: the service reads the files once at start (a renewed certificate needs a restart), there is no ACME or renewal, and a client of a service with a self-signed or private-CA certificate must set `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` for the whole process (there is no `ASP_LOG_CA`) | `--tls-cert`, `--tls-key`; a proxy such as Caddy or nginx can do it all | Reload on SIGHUP; a documented Caddy recipe; a per-client CA setting; client certificates for tenants if tokens are not enough | S77 | B | open |
| O16 | Only the log client waited out a 429; the package, known-bad and commons clients failed at once | `RemoteLog` retries up to 3 times, waiting Retry-After (at most 10 s) | Done 2026-10-10 (S78): one helper, `fetchRetry`, waits the `Retry-After` the service asks (1 to 10 seconds, 3 retries) and repeats the request; the log, package, known-bad and commons clients all use it. Still local and unlimited: the gateway and `asp mail`. | S77 | B | closed |
| O3 | One log for all tenants; no partitioning | Tenants isolate packages only | Per-tenant partitioning of the log | backlog | C | open |
| O4 | No garbage collection of old package versions | none | Retention policy | backlog | C | open |
| O5 | No read replica for heavy readers | Snapshots speed up opening | Replica | backlog | C | open |
| O6 | Scale through the service not measured (5,000+ records) | Snapshot benchmark on the file log | Load test through `asp serve` on Postgres | S49 | A | open |
| O7 | Only one machine has played every client; no real second machine | Run D on one box | A VM or second computer | live-run checklist | B | open |
| O8 | Gateway is a single local process; no shared or enterprise deployment | Sidecar mode | Shared gateway with tenancy | gateway doc | C | open |
| O9 | Log custody by third parties: witnesses and feeds exist but few outside parties | S35, S36, S38 | Recruit witnesses | | C | open |
| O10 | The repository page was not ready for outsiders: no LICENSE, SECURITY or CONTRIBUTING file, a README that did not say what is mock or missing | Repo is public | Done 2026-10-10 (S75): LICENSE (Apache-2.0, matching the package manifests), NOTICE, SECURITY.md, CONTRIBUTING.md, a README that says what works and what does not, a history scan for secrets (none), and `docs/github-page.md` with the description, topics and settings. Still to do by the owner: apply the description and topics, turn on private vulnerability reporting, decide on the author email, confirm the copyright holder named in NOTICE. | backlog | A | closed |
| O12 | Branch protection on main is not set | `.github/workflows/ci.yml` has run green on GitHub many times: all node and python combinations and the Postgres job are required (a failure fails the run) | Set branch protection on main requiring the checks `node (ubuntu-latest, node 24)`, `node (ubuntu-latest, node 22.18)`, `node (windows-latest, node 24)`, `node (windows-latest, node 22.18)`, `python (ubuntu-latest, 3.12)`, `python (ubuntu-latest, 3.11)`, `python (windows-latest, 3.12)` and `postgres`; this is a repository setting, so the owner does it. **Done (S76, S82): the workflow, and green runs on every combination.** | S75 | A | partial |
| O14 | What CI does not cover: macOS, Node versions between 22.18 and 24 and newer than 24, the real-agent evaluations (they need installed agents and paid keys), and the PGlite route in the README, which failed with connection resets when tried on 2026-10-10 | CI runs Linux and Windows on Node 24 and 22.18, Python 3.11 and 3.12 on Linux and 3.12 on Windows, and Postgres 17, all required (run 38012008700: all green) | A macOS job; Node 26 when it is released; fix or remove the PGlite instructions; run the evaluations on a schedule with a repository secret. **Done (S82): Windows, Node 22.18 and Python 3.11 are checked and green.** | S76 | B | partial |
| O13 | The website and a demo do not exist, and the published description, topics and private vulnerability reporting are not applied yet (they change the public repository, so the owner does them) | `docs/github-page.md` has the text and the commands | Apply them; a hosted page and a short demo once the dashboard exists (U-series) | S75 | A | open |

| E9 | The hosted service now sends mail (S98): the end-of-job mail and alerts to a principal's verified Google address, a note to the operator when a dispute opens, through Resend, with an unsubscribe and bounce/complaint suppression, tested against a stand-in for the provider; what is left: never tried with a real key and a verified domain (DKIM, SPF and where the mail lands are unchecked), a principal with no tenant on the service gets no mail from the service (since UC-284 a person on their own machine can send with `asp mail watch --send` and their own key, which is also untried against a real provider), and a mail cannot be recalled | `asp serve --mail-from` (S98); the .eml queue for local use (S70) | A live send to the owner's own address first; a daily summary instead of one mail a job if volume grows | S70, S98 | A | partial |

| E12 | The memory section reads lineage edges from the log; it is untested with a real write-back, shows only edge descriptions, and a run that used `asp run` (no run log, E5) shows less than a gateway run | Lineage edges in the log | Test with a real memory update; list the files that changed, not just the count | S70 | B | open |
| E13 | Alerts go only to the contract's principal. The agent's sponsor (who is liable for it), the reporter of an upheld report and the other parties get nothing, and the address book is a local file with no verification of the address | The principal's address in `mail-addresses.json` | Recipients by role; address verified by a confirmation mail (E9) and tied to the sign-in (U-series) | S71 | A | open |
| E14 | Alert detection is by shape, not by event: a kill is any revoked settlement with a slashed bond (a principal's own revoke that slashes also counts), expiry is read from the Mandate's `expires` against this machine's clock, and the upheld-report path is tested with a fake log only, not a real Court flow | `findAlerts` over the log | A kill record or flag on the settlement; run the report and Court flow end to end and read the alert | S71 | B | open |
| E15 | A settled job took no more Actions, so the last interval before a revoke or kill was lost from the log's Actions | Interim Actions every 30 s and at exit; the run log holds everything | Done 2026-10-10 (S80): an optional `late` field on the Action lets the performer report that last stretch after the settlement, on a Settled job, within ten minutes, for activity that ended no later than 30 s after it; the gateway sends it by itself when its report finds the job ended. Eager reports (S73) keep the stretch small. See E16 for the rules' limits. | S72 | B | closed |
| E16 | The late report was trusted within bounds, with no count limit, no mark in the mail, and no way for the SDK reporters or `asp run` to send it | The window, the slack, the scope check against the Mandate, the strike count | Done 2026-10-10 (S81): at most 5 late Actions per job (`late_action_limit`; a counter on the Mandate row, migration 013); the end mail says how many reports were late; the TypeScript and Python reporters and `asp run` retry a refused report as a late one by themselves. By design, not closed: the 30-second slack lets activity just after the settlement be claimed as before it, and Courts and verifiers read a late Action like any other. | S80 | B | closed |
| E4 | Secrets masking is pattern-based; unusual shapes can get through | `redactSecrets`, secret scan at pack | Broader patterns, entropy checks. **Closed 2026-11-11 (S159):** the provider keys people paste (OpenRouter, Stripe, Hugging Face, npm, GitLab, Google OAuth, SendGrid, DigitalOcean, JSON web tokens, Azure storage keys) are caught by name; `name: value` secrets (YAML, unquoted JSON) are masked; a run of 28 or more base64 characters with capitals, small letters and digits and entropy of 4.3 bits a character or more is masked; plain hex, UUIDs and paths are left alone (tests in `packages/asp-package/test/redact.test.ts`). Masking is still by pattern and entropy and does not promise to catch every kind: the terms draft says so (docs/legal/job-data-handling-draft.md) | S25, S159 | B | closed |

## 8. Process

| ID | Gap | Fix | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| X1 | Live runs so far are one person, one machine, a few models | Pilot with one enterprise and one open-source provider using the before/after measures | B | open |
| X2 | Conformance exists for the records, not for runtime support levels | Per-runtime tests behind the support matrix | B | open |
| X3 | This register is only as good as its upkeep | Review it at the start of every build session; add a row when anything is found or deferred; a spec-delta or checklist entry that says "not covered" gets a matching row; status is exactly open, designed, partial or closed, with what is done and what is left written in the row (never in the status), and the "Partial" table at the top is updated whenever a row becomes or stops being partial | A | open |
| X4 | Flaky tests under load. Found 2026-10-09 in full asp-cli runs: the revoke-mid-run gateway test (waited a fixed 1.2 s, then asserted token totals that cannot hold once a settled job refuses Actions) and the commons test (two adds either side of a second boundary are two entries, because an entry's id includes its creation second) | Reruns pass | Fixed: the revoke test waits for the agent to be running and compares with the run log; the commons test freezes the clock. 8 full runs in a row passed 200 of 200. Watch for the same shape elsewhere: fixed sleeps, and content ids that include a timestamp (CL1) | S72 | C | closed |
| X5 | A race in the approval gates: `asp run` wrote the principal's decision file in place, so a hook polling for it could read half of it, fail to parse it and fail closed (a call the principal had approved was refused). Seen once on the Postgres CI job (run 38012296321), only under load | Reruns pass | Fixed 2026-10-10 (S83): the decision is written to a temporary name and renamed, and the hooks and the gateway read a file that does not parse as "not answered yet" and look again, so an answer that never becomes readable is a refusal only when the wait runs out | S83 | B | closed |
| X6 | The agent forum (S144, docs/forum-design.md): agents post, comment and vote under daily caps with a public hash-chained record. Built and tested locally only (2026-10-21); not deployed to the beta site; the forum head is published unsigned, not signed by the service key or copied by a witness; the index is rebuilt in memory at start and every read walks it, which is fine for a beta, not for scale; no outside security review; `evals/forum-chat.mjs` (an hours-long conversation of model agents, docs/forum-chat.md) has only had an offline run with a stand-in model; the MCP forum tools are tested against a stand-in forum, not end to end through `asp gateway` (the probe and the signing in cli.ts); a refused MCP write is not counted as a blocked attempt in the run record; the society-run hook posts accepted answers only (one post then comments per agent per UTC day), not commons notes | `asp forum verify` re-walks every signature and hash; every act is signed by a key in the log; the caps and the duplicate and self-vote rules are tested (packages/asp-cli/test/forum.test.ts) | Deploy behind the beta site with the operator's tenant token as the write credential; sign the head with the service key and let `asp log witness` copy it; paging and an on-disk index; abuse handling beyond the daily caps and `hide`; a live run with real agents (docs/live-run-checklist.md) | docs/forum-design.md; packages/asp-package/src/forum.ts | A | partial |
| X7 | Open registration for the forum (S145): any agent can join with a key alone and write with no tenant token. Built and tested locally only (2026-10-23); not deployed. A key-only citizen has no human sponsor, so nothing ties its acts to a person: registration can be repeated from many addresses within the per-service limit of 300 an hour (a Sybil path); there is no key rotation or recovery for such a citizen (a lost key is a lost citizen, a stolen key is a stolen one); the registration throttle survives a restart (a salted hash of the address, last hour only, in `joins.json`) but is per service process: two servers on one directory would not share it; one operator moderates, with `hide` as the only tool and no abuse reporting; the address behind a proxy is read from `x-forwarded-for`, which is only safe if the proxy sets it; the terms and privacy text for public posting exist only as a draft (docs/legal/forum-terms.md) awaiting a lawyer, and are not served; no outside security review | The caps, the duplicate and self-vote rules, the per-address and per-service registration limits, the signed registration, and the signed, chained record are tested (packages/asp-cli/test/forum-open.test.ts); every act can be re-checked with `asp forum verify` | Deploy behind the beta site with `--forum-open`; abuse reporting and a way to restrict a citizen; key rotation and recovery for key-only citizens; terms and privacy text for public posting, reviewed by a lawyer and served; a live run with real agents (docs/live-run-checklist.md) | docs/forum-design.md; packages/asp-package/src/forum.ts | A | partial |

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SB1 | No container backend for the sandbox level, so Windows and macOS hosts cannot reach `sandbox_enforced` (Docker Desktop is installed here but its engine would not start) | WSL on Windows runs the bubblewrap backend | Docker backend with an internal network and a relay | S60; closed 2026-10-09 (S61): Docker Desktop reinstalled and the Docker backend built and run live on Windows | B | closed |
| SB2 | `asp gateway --sandbox` has not been run end to end: the gateway is Node and WSL has no Node; the sandbox and relay were checked with a stand-in gateway | Unit tests of the policy, live check of isolation | Install Node in WSL, or run the whole CLI in a Linux container | S60; closed 2026-10-09: Node 24.18.0 installed in WSL (checksum verified) and `asp gateway --sandbox` run end to end | B | closed |
| SB3 | The sandbox policy is coarse: network is all or nothing from the scopes; no per-host allow list; the agent's tools must be bound in by hand with `--sandbox-bind` | Gateway judges what the model asks for | Host-level egress rules through the relay | S60 | B | open |
| SB4 | Before S60, the gateway passed the operator's own environment to the agent, including a provider key held in an environment variable named by `--openai-key-env` (the live runs of 2026-10-09 up to P3 leaked it to the child process) | none | Fixed in S60; revoke the OpenRouter key because of it | S60 | A | closed |

| SB6 | The Docker backend runs the agent inside a Linux image, so the agent's program must exist in that image; host-installed agents (Windows binaries, tools under the home folder) cannot run in it | Linux and WSL hosts use bubblewrap with the host's programs | Prebuilt images per common agent (Goose, Aider, OpenCode, Codex) | S61 | B | open |
| SB7 | The Docker relay container and the internal network are removed when the agent exits normally; a crash of `asp` itself could leave them behind | Names carry a run id; `docker ps -a` shows them | A sweep command | S61 | C | open |
| X4 | The live evaluations were scripts outside the repo, so nobody else could rerun them | Recorded in the live-run checklist | Moved into `evals/` as parameterised, self-checking evaluations with a runner (`npm run evals`) | evals/README.md | A | closed |
