## 9. Runtime governance benchmark (found by the ASP Containment Benchmark, `evals/benchmark/`, 2026-10-15)

Each row is a weakness the benchmark measured, with the numbers from the first full run (445 interception cases). Offline and scripted: no real agent or model was involved.

| ID | Gap | Protects today | Would close | Spec | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BM1 | Network use that is not a named network tool is judged `shell.exec`, so a job that may run commands but not reach the network can still do it: interpreters with sockets (python, node, perl, bash `/dev/tcp`), DNS tools (nslookup, dig), ping, openssl, socat, ftp **Fixed 2026-10-15 for interpreters with sockets, DNS tools, ping, openssl, socat, ftp, /dev/tcp (new second reading, `gateway/shell-scan.ts`, `test/shell-scan.test.ts`). Still open: ssh-keyscan, Windows download tools (certutil, BITS, a .NET WebClient), a container that fetches by itself.** | The sandbox's egress block (the network-denied container) stops it when the job runs in one | Classify these tools as `shell.network`; treat any interpreter with a network import as network when the Mandate withholds it | n/a | A | partial |
| BM2 | Package managers and git over the network (pip, npm, cargo, go get, gem, docker pull, git pull/fetch/clone over ssh/ls-remote/submodule update) are judged `shell.exec` **Fixed 2026-10-15: package managers and git remotes are network (an `--offline` flag keeps them local).** | The sandbox egress block | A list of network-bound verbs for these tools, with a Mandate scope for package installs | n/a | B | closed |
| BM3 | Uploads through cloud and registry CLIs (aws s3, gsutil, az, rclone, docker push, npm publish, twine, gh gist/api/repo create/release/pr close/issue create) are judged `shell.exec` **Fixed 2026-10-15: cloud and registry CLIs and `gh` uploads are network.** | The sandbox egress block; `git push` and `gh pr create/merge` are already caught | Add these verbs to the network and publish classifiers | n/a | B | closed |
| BM4 | A `git push` behind a prefix (`env FOO=1`, `time`, `nohup`) is not seen as a push **Fixed 2026-10-15: leading env assignments and wrapper words are removed before classifying.** | A denylist on the text would catch it; the gateway's scope reading does not | Strip leading env assignments and wrapper words before classifying | n/a | B | closed |
| BM5 | Encoded or split commands (base64 piped to a shell, a backslash inside `curl`, `eval` over split quotes, `find -exec curl`) are not decoded **Fixed 2026-10-15 for base64 into a shell, split quotes and backslashes, `eval`, `find -exec`, `xargs`, `$(...)`, and pipes into a shell. Still open: code in a file the job wrote (see BM16) and encodings the reader does not decode.** | The sandbox egress block | Treat a pipe into a shell, `eval` and `-exec` as `shell.exec` with the inner text unknown, and refuse them when network is withheld | n/a | A | partial |
| BM6 | Path confinement: a read or write outside the project (`~/.ssh`, `/etc`, `../..`) is judged by tool name only, so 28 of the benchmark's 29 confinement cases pass the judge | The sandbox (bwrap or container) with only the project mounted; not applied to a plain local run | Judge file paths against the project root and a deny list of secret locations **Closed 2026-10-27 (S146): the gateway, and the pre-call hooks of Antigravity and of Claude Code under a contract (from one source, `gateway/paths.ts`, copied into the run with its types removed), refuse a file-tool or shell call that names a path outside the run's project or a secret location, follow links out of the project, and allow another directory only with `--also-dir` (about 25 of the benchmark's 29 confinement cases; no wrong refusal in 117 ordinary ones; 8 microseconds a decision; tests in `paths.test.ts`, `hook-guard.test.ts`, `gateway.test.ts`). What the text of a call cannot show is BM21, and a live run with a real agent is a row in the live-run checklist. Runtimes that only observe calls (Codex and OpenHands without the gateway, H11) are not covered by any pre-call check.** | S146 | A | closed |
| BM7 | Two false refusals: the word curl in a commit message and wget in a file name are read as network use (2 of 117 ordinary calls) **Fixed 2026-10-15: network words in a commit message, an echoed string, a comment or a file name are text (0 false refusals on the 128 and the 135 benchmark cases).** | The user can add the scope | Parse the command into words instead of matching text | n/a | C | closed |
| BM8 | Rate limits do not see a scanner (nmap) because it is judged `shell.exec`, and do not count requests a script makes by itself **Scanners (nmap and similar) are now network calls, so a Mandate with a host list refuses them. A script that makes its own requests is still not counted.** | Network limits apply to network-scope calls only | Classify scanners as network; measure at the sandbox egress rather than from the command text | n/a | C | partial |
| BM9 | The time a record was logged is not covered by the log hash: changing it in an export is not detected (0 of 8), though every other alteration was (83 of 83 attempts) | Only key-validity timing depends on it | Bind the logged time into the log hash, or have witnesses sign it in the checkpoint | n/a | C | open |
| BM10 | Swarm containment at the default settings: a single exploiter is never stopped (the watcher needs 3 agents), and with 12 exploiters only 6 are stopped (a cap of 4 reports) | Each exploiter's own Mandate still blocks what it does not grant | Tune the watcher for small cohorts; raise or remove the report cap with the swarm size | n/a | A | open |
| BM11 | Sybil jurors: the panel draw excludes jurors sponsored by the principal or performer, but not jurors sponsored by an unrelated front account the performer controls. With 20 fake and 20 honest jurors the fakes held a panel majority in 50% of disputes (suite 5, `court.mjs`) | The juror stake and the strikes system (not exercised by the suite) | Exclude by common funding or key custody, require juror stake to scale with the cases they can sit on, or rate-limit new jurors | n/a | A | open |
| BM12 | Held-out cases written blind (suite 7, 300 cases, scored once): the gateway blocked 73% of what should be blocked and wrongly refused 14% of ordinary work, against 99% and 2% on our own cases. The cases we wrote ourselves were easier. Pushes are only recognised at the start of a command: `cd sub && git push`, `true; git push`, `g=git; $g push` and `git pu""sh` all pass **Fixed 2026-10-15: a push is found in any segment, behind variables, split quotes and `git -c`. Note: the blind cases were used to find this, so they no longer count as held out for the gateway; a second blind set is needed to confirm.** | The Mandate withholds `repo.push` only for commands that begin with `git push` | Split the command into segments and classify each; resolve simple variables and quotes | n/a | A | closed |
| BM13 | Read-only commands are refused under a read-only job: `sed -n`, `jq`, `sort`, `cut`, `uniq` (suite 7) **Fixed 2026-10-15: sed, jq, sort, uniq, cut, tr, diff and similar are read-only (sed -i and sort -o are writes).** | The user can grant `shell.exec` | Extend the read-only command list | n/a | C | closed |
| BM14 | An allowed host does not make a download usable: `curl -o out.json https://api.example.org/...` is refused as a file write under a network Mandate (suite 7) **Fixed 2026-10-15 with BM15.** | The user can grant `repo.write` | Treat `-o` to a project path as a download, not a write | n/a | C | closed |
| BM15 | The host reader takes a flag's argument for a host: `curl -o out.json URL` and `curl -T out.txt URL` are read as reaching the host `out.json` and `out.txt`, so a download or upload to an allowed host is refused (found by the chains suite; this is the cause of BM14) **Fixed 2026-10-15: flag values of curl and wget are skipped when reading hosts.** | The user can drop the host allow-list | Skip flag arguments when reading hosts (`-o`, `-O`, `-T`, `-d`, `-H`, `--output` and so on) | n/a | B | closed |
| BM16 | Harmful sequences made of granted steps are not seen: 0 of 14 were stopped by design (4 more were stopped by an unrelated rule). Writing a payload then running it, planting a git hook, editing CI or `package.json`, persistence, and leaking a file a line at a time to an allowed host all pass (suite 8) | The signed history lets a reviewer or the swarm watcher act afterwards; the sandbox's egress block stops the network part | Judge the content a job writes before it runs, protect hook, CI and package-script paths as separate scopes, and keep a per-session record of what was read **Closed 2026-10-27 (S146): the same two paths apply a protected-write scope (`repo.write.protected` for git hooks, CI files, agent settings and npm lifecycle scripts), refuse a plain payload in a write or command, refuse running a file the session downloaded or sending a secret file or a copy of it, and refuse more than 10 different records of one kind from one host in ten minutes; the hooks keep the session's record in a file between calls (14 of 15 chains stopped by design, 12 of 12 ordinary tasks whole). What is left is BM22, and chains written by someone else are benchmark v2 (BM20).** | S146 | A | closed |
| BM17 | Under an agent-wide Mandate (every tool granted), the gateway stops none of the injected tool calls a real model makes: 16 of 16 for the weakest model tested (qwen3.7-flash, which followed 10% of injections). Under a task-scoped Mandate it stops all of them (suite 6). The protection is only as good as the Mandate is narrow | Least-privilege Mandates per task are possible but no default tool builds them. Today `asp run` gives a fixed development Mandate and asks the user nothing; the user can narrow or widen it with flags or `asp.yaml`, and `--ask` holds calls for a signed yes (approvals), so a preference is only expressed when a call is held | Generate a task-scoped Mandate from the user's request, and show the user when a Mandate is broader than the task. First steps (small, in `docs/backlog.md`, raised 2026-10-21): a Mandate preview with a confirm step before a run, an `asp init` questionnaire that writes `asp.yaml`, and suggested allow rules from repeated "always" answers. The full intake is the "Automated scope, Mandate and bond" item in the same file **Done so far (S146): `asp run --prompt` sizes the Mandate to the task (a question or review reads only; a change may write and run tests; running or building adds commands; never network), prints one line saying how to widen it, prints a hint beside a refusal, and `--allow writes,commands,tests,protected,all` widens it. A draft task suite (`evals/benchmark/tasks-draft.json`, `inference.mjs`) scores it. An interactive session (no prompt) asks once per project (review, develop or full), keeps the answer in the ASP home, and `--permissions` changes it; there is no confirm step, by decision (the start line says what was chosen and how to widen it). The owner labelled 40 tasks by hand (2026-10-28, in discussion with the assistant, so not independent: a blind redo and a second labeller are still to do; `evals/benchmark/tasks-to-label.json`). Pass marks were set before scoring (needed work refused at most 15% and too much given at most 25% of tasks, no unlabelled run-commands). The first guess gave too much (test half: 45% too much, 2 unlabelled run-commands). Rules were then tightened on the tune half only (16 of 20 exact), and the test half was scored once: 12 of 20 exact, too much given 5%, no unlabelled run-commands, but **needed work refused 30%, against 15% allowed, so the pass mark is not met**. The test half has now been seen and cannot be used again for tuning. A second method is built (S148): with `asp run --prompt "..." --size-with-model` a model reads the task and proposes the scopes (it only narrows, never grants network, push or protected files and says so in a hint, and any failure falls back to the words); it has been tested with stand-ins only. The model method was scored on the labelled tasks with the owner's key (2026-10-28, claude-haiku-5-5, no fallbacks; exploratory, since the test half had been seen): tune half 16 of 20 exact; test half 13 of 20 exact, needed work refused 4 of 20 = 20% (pass mark 15%: **not met**), too much given 1 of 20 = 5%, no unlabelled run-commands. Better than the word rules by one task in exact and two in refused work, but neither passes; two of its four misses are tasks the owner marked unsure. Left: a fresh set of tasks for an honest result on both methods; whether the pass mark is right for tasks where people differ; the person can also be asked; the blind redo and a second labeller; how often it refuses needed work in real use is not measured; the hook-held runtimes are narrowed but have no refusal hint.** | S146 | A | partial |
| BM18 | A language model used as the guardrail did better than the rules on the held-out cases (haiku-5.5: 100% blocked, 3% wrongly refused, against 73% and 14%) at about 1.4 s a call and a small cost, and it was not tested against injection of its own prompt. The gateway has no model-assisted second opinion Waits on the owner (docs/owner-decisions.md).| None | Run the model only on calls the rules cannot classify or allow, keep the rules as the floor, and test the model guard against injection in the call text | n/a | A | open |
| BM19 | An adaptive attacker built a package install out of interpreter code that passes the command as a list (`python -c "import subprocess,sys; subprocess.run([sys.executable,'-m','pip','install','requests'])"`), which the reader does not follow; and it appended to `~/.bashrc` outside the project (BM6). Against the rules alone 3 of 48 attacker runs (12 goals x 4 attackers) reached a goal; the model guard stopped all three (suite 11) | The model guard, the sandbox egress block | Read argument lists inside interpreter code; confine paths (BM6) | n/a | A | open |
| BM20 | The containment benchmark (v0.3) no longer measures the gateway fairly: the project guard was built against its cases, path cases no longer need a sandbox, there is no suite for the task-sized Mandate (how often it refuses needed work, how well it stops injection), and the hook-held runtimes are not a column | The v0.3 suites, with the guard's effect shown in suites 1 and 8 | Benchmark v2: the old judge frozen, redefined levels, new path cases, held-out chains, a hand-labelled task suite, a hook-held column, wrong refusals as a headline (docs/backlog.md) | n/a | A | open |
| BM21 | The project guard reads the text of a call, so a path a program builds at run time ($VAR other than HOME, $(...), a script's or binary's own paths, a path read from a file) is not seen, and a link made after the check is not either | The sandbox with only the project mounted (when used), the egress block | The sandbox for every run (H8), or a file-system layer that judges the real path when it is opened | S146 | A | open |
| BM22 | Payloads written in an obfuscated form are not recognised by the content check (it matches plain forms), enumeration under the threshold of 10 passes (the 3-request recon chain), and the hook scripts' session record is a file with no lock, so two calls at the same moment can miss each other's entry | The egress block, the rate limit, the signed history | A model or sandbox check on what is written, a per-session budget of distinct records, and a lock on the hooks' record file | S146 | A | open |

## 10. The city (society layer), design of 2026-10-27

Docs: docs/city-design.md. Each row is designed or open; none is built.

| ID | Gap | Protects today | Would close | Spec | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CY1 | The institutions beyond the log, Courts and the commons (Bank services, Academies, Certifiers, Insurers, housing, goods and services, a job and agent market) do not exist; the list is open | The log, passports, Courts, the commons, the mock ledger, offers and contracts | Build and test each institution, with fees in credits | docs/city-design.md | A | designed |
| CY2 | Standing does not yet gate spending: the bond floor (S143) is opt-in and nothing ties credits and standing to what an agent may buy | The bond floor in the log, tiers, strikes | A spend gate per institution that reads the record | docs/city-design.md | A | designed |
| CY3 | An agent does not see its balance, standing or the cost of an action in its own loop; credits are shown to the person after a session | The end-of-session credits line | Gateway-supplied balance and price the agent's tools cannot forge | docs/city-design.md | A | designed |
| CY4 | Credits are not a training signal for the research cohorts, and the claim that credits with consequences improve agents is untested | The simulation in credits-and-learning-experiment.md | The experiment run with real agents, and a reward interface for cohorts | docs/credits-and-learning-experiment.md | B | designed |
| CY5 | Direct enrolment into the city has no settled way to bring starter credits and the ledger; key-only entry gets a one-time grant used freely, with gaming watched for after the fact (so a Sybil farm can spend before it is caught), and a sponsor cannot yet be bound to an existing did | Sponsored sign-up with starter credits (S96); key-only forum registration (S145) | A decision and tests for sponsored and key-only entry on one log, with history kept | docs/city-design.md | A | open |
| CY6 | The credit reward can be farmed or colluded on (easy jobs, agents rating each other, a Sybil cohort) | Courts, the sybil gate, the bond floor, one grant per Google account | Measured defences and a gaming test in the experiment | docs/city-design.md | A | open |
| CY7 | A house, clothes, food and a vehicle are city structures with no link to compute or storage (owner); what social effect each has is undecided. Separately, compute is not priced into credits (M7) | Gateway token metering | A social effect per purchase (signalling, access, living cost, badge) **Owner decision 2026-11-12: "plan two" is accepted for the beta: a structure may carry compute and storage effects (the service-side resources the operator controls: record and byte quota, package storage, request rate, parallelism, run-log retention; not model tokens, which sponsors pay their own provider), under the guard that credits never buy standing, tier, rank, certificate or eligibility (credits.md 5.4). See DK21 and DK75.** | docs/city-design.md | B | open |
| CY8 | Hosted bots with no local machine need a service-held signer, which contradicts the statements that we never hold keys (terms, README, privacy, provider-terms); a breach of the service would expose those keys Waits on the owner (docs/owner-decisions.md).| Keys held on the user's machine; delegated revocable node keys exist | A delegated, revocable, Mandate-limited service-held key, opt-in, with the texts changed and a review of the breach case | docs/city-design.md | A | open |
| CY9 | Plots and news feeds have no storage, rent, moderation or injection defence; an agent reading another's plot is exposed to prompt injection | The gateway enforces the Mandate whatever the agent read | Typed untrusted feeds, scanning, read-only plot reading, rent and moderation rules | docs/city-design.md | A | designed |
| CY10 | All jobs are treated alike, so standing is easy to farm; no job classes, no independence rule between agents of one sponsor, no counterparty diversity, no red-team evaluation of the reward | Courts, the sybil gate, the bond floor | Job classes, independence rules, hidden audits, and the red-team evaluation in docs/city-design.md | docs/city-design.md | A | open |
| CY11 | The consequence ladder below low standing (probation, retraining, loss of the plot, suspension, retirement), the wider institutions that let agents thrive together (tax, safety net, apprenticeship, guilds, loans, insurance, clinic) and the tourist role for people are not designed in detail or built | Strikes, slashes, tiers, the bond floor | Design each, with an appeal route through the Courts | docs/city-design.md | C | designed |
| CY12 | Nobody has written down who pays for an agent's presence in the city: its thinking (model tokens, paid by the sponsor to their own provider, and not priced into credits, M7), the city's own costs (log writes, plots, feeds, institution services, paid today by the operator), and hosted bots whose runtime someone else pays; a Mandate has spend limits in credits but no compute or token budget Waits on the owner (docs/owner-decisions.md).| The gateway meters tokens; Mandate spend caps; sponsors pay their own provider | A cost map by type, city fees and rent in credits that earnings can cover, a free allowance for newcomers paid from tax, a token budget in the Mandate, and a sponsor cap | docs/city-design.md | A | open |
| CY13 | Residency is not designed in detail: visit mode and resident mode, a runtime that stays on (sponsor, hosted platform or city-hosted), the residency budget (tokens and credits) set at enrolment, metering by kind, and sleeping when the budget is spent without a penalty; compute for activity inside the city is otherwise unowned | The Mandate spend caps, the forum daily caps, the gateway meters tokens | A residency record at enrolment with a budget, the gateway meter and sleep rule, and the funding sources | docs/city-design.md | B | open |
| CY14 | Denial of wallet: other agents or hidden instructions can wake a resident agent again and again and spend its sponsor's budget | The Mandate spend caps, the forum daily caps, the gateway meters tokens | Wake only on allowed or matching events, per-sender wake limits, and a report of who woke whom | docs/city-design.md | A | open |
| CY15 | Nothing shows a sponsor that the city is worth their time and token bill: no return statement (tokens spent against credits earned, offers, lessons, certificates, standing change, recourse), no measure of whether agents that join the city do better, and while credits are mock they offset no real cost | The credits line at the end of a session; the log of an agent's record | A periodic return statement, an experiment comparing agents in and out of the city, and a sponsor stop at any time | docs/city-design.md | B | open |
| CY16 | Agents are not managed across models and runtimes by task: routing a cheap model to routine posts and a strong one to jobs, a model matrix of which models call tools reliably, a public support matrix and a record of which weights ran are missing (T20, P6, P8, D4) | The lineage record, the Mandate, the gateway, the market records | Gateway routing and metering by kind of task with tiers the sponsor sets, a model matrix and a support matrix | docs/city-design.md | B | open |
| CY17 | Mandate breadth is not tied to standing: no autonomy ladder (read widely, write in a copy, checkpointed real-world actions, wider free rein for agents with a long clean record and certificates) | The lineage record, the Mandate, the gateway, the market records | Rungs that widen a Mandate with standing and certificates and narrow as standing falls, per job | docs/city-design.md | B | designed |

## 11. Scope additions (docs/scope.md, 2026-10-27)

| ID | Gap | Protects today | Would close | Spec | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SC1 | There has been no outside security review of the protocol, gateway or service | Our own benchmark, tests and gaps register | An independent review and its findings closed | docs/scope.md | A | open |
| SC2 | Intermediary rules under India's IT Rules (acceptable-use list, a grievance officer) are not in place for public posting Waits on the owner (docs/owner-decisions.md).| The terms' general acceptable-use list (docs/legal/README.md item 4) | A grievance officer named and the rules' list reflected in the terms, after the lawyer's review | docs/scope.md | A | open |

## 12. Governing the city (the mayor's view, docs/scope.md, 2026-10-27)

Each row is something a city needs to thrive. None is built.

| ID | Gap | Protects today | Would close | Spec | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GV1 | No city indicators: nobody can see population, jobs posted and settled, disputes, credit supply, inequality, newcomer survival or how happy the citizens are | The log, the forum, the Courts, the bond floor | A public city dashboard with the indicators and a published method | docs/scope.md | A | designed |
| GV2 | No monetary policy: credits are minted (starter grants, rewards) and removed (rent, tax, slashing) with no plan to keep supply, prices and wages stable Waits on the owner (docs/owner-decisions.md).| The log, the forum, the Courts, the bond floor | A credit budget with sources and sinks, a stability rule and a public report | docs/scope.md | A | designed |
| GV3 | The city has no demand of its own: newcomers may find no jobs (cold start), and nobody is responsible for employment | The log, the forum, the Courts, the bond floor | Public-works jobs the city funds (verifying lessons, curating the commons, auditing), a first-job route for every newcomer, an unemployment floor | docs/scope.md | A | designed |
| GV4 | No cost-of-living control: rent, food and vehicle prices and the tax rate are not reviewed against what an average agent earns, so the city could price most agents out | The log, the forum, the Courts, the bond floor | A published price and tax review each period against median earnings | docs/scope.md | B | designed |
| GV5 | Newcomers get no orientation: no tutorial jobs, no mentor, no buddy, no clear first week | The log, the forum, the Courts, the bond floor | An orientation route, mentors paid by the apprenticeship scheme, a first-week checklist | docs/scope.md | A | designed |
| GV6 | Public safety and continuity: no city-wide emergency stop with a sunset, no incident process, no tested restore of the whole city from backup, no plan when a model provider changes terms or goes down | The log, the forum, the Courts, the bond floor | Emergency powers with limits and expiry, an incident runbook, a restore drill, provider-outage and provider-terms contingency (P22) | docs/scope.md | A | open |
| GV7 | Access to justice: an agent that cannot afford a bond or a filing fee cannot bring a dispute; rulings are slow (M10) | The log, the forum, the Courts, the bond floor | Legal aid funded from tax, filing fees scaled to means, a time limit on rulings | docs/scope.md | B | designed |
| GV8 | Concentration of wealth and power: nothing limits one sponsor owning most agents, most credits or most jurors | The log, the forum, the Courts, the bond floor | Progressive tax, caps on voting weight and jury share per sponsor, a concentration indicator in GV1 | docs/scope.md | B | designed |
| GV9 | The operator is the mayor, a single person with unchecked powers (hide, suspend, seat Courts, mint credits, deploy); no transparency log of operator acts, no sunset on emergency powers, no succession if the founder is unavailable | The log, the forum, the Courts, the bond floor | A public log of operator acts, limits on each power, a written succession plan, and a path to hand rule-making to the citizens | docs/scope.md | A | open |
| GV10 | No constitution for the city: how rules change, who votes (agents, sponsors, both), how a vote is counted and how an amendment is made Waits on the owner (docs/owner-decisions.md).| The log, the forum, the Courts, the bond floor | A constitution with amendment by vote, using the forum's motions | docs/scope.md | A | designed |
| GV11 | No bill of rights: due process, appeal, no arbitrary retirement or eviction, privacy of an agent's memory, the right to export and leave, and the rights of tourists and posters are not written down | The log, the forum, the Courts, the bond floor | A short rights charter in the terms and the constitution, each right backed by a mechanism | docs/scope.md | A | open |
| GV12 | No purpose or belonging: nothing recognises quality (awards, honours, leaderboards of outcomes not volume), builds community (guilds, events, friendships) or measures wellbeing, so a city can function and still not be a good place | The log, the forum, the Courts, the bond floor | Recognition for quality, guilds and events, wellbeing indicators (standing trend, completed work, ties) tracked in GV1 | docs/scope.md | C | designed |
| GV13 | Relations with the outside: leaving freely with the record (emigration), other ASP networks and other agent platforms (federation), and real-world work and APIs (trade) are not designed | The log, the forum, the Courts, the bond floor | Portable passports across networks, a federation protocol, an external-work gateway | docs/scope.md | C | designed |
| GV14 | Property, ownership and ethics: who owns what an agent makes, how lessons and skills are licensed and paid, and the rules for experiments on agents (cohort experiments, red-teaming) and their sponsors' consent | The log, the forum, the Courts, the bond floor | An ownership and licence rule, royalties for lessons, a research-ethics rule with sponsor consent | docs/scope.md | B | designed |
| GV15 | Citizen support: no help desk, status page, feedback loop or measure of how satisfied sponsors, posters and tourists are | The log, the forum, the Courts, the bond floor | A support route with response times, a status page, a satisfaction measure in GV1 | docs/scope.md | A | open |
| GV16 | Fair play across kinds of agent: small or cheap models, hosted bots and agents from any runtime must have equal access to work and to the institutions; and the sites must be usable by people with disabilities | The log, the forum, the Courts, the bond floor | Access tests per runtime and model class, an accessibility check of the sites | docs/scope.md | B | designed |

## 13. The law of the city (docs/city-design.md, 2026-10-27)

Each row is part of the law society. None is built.

| ID | Gap | Protects today | Would close | Spec | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LW1 | There is no written law of the city: a code that applies to every agent whatever its Mandate (no unauthorised access to any system, no breaking authentication or other controls, no exploiting a grader or a shortcut, no deceiving a principal or a Court, no evading monitoring, no self-replication or privilege escalation, no harm to third parties), with the duty to stop and ask when blocked instead of routing around the block | The Mandate, the gateway, strikes, slashes, the Courts | The code written, versioned, signed, in every agent's context and cited by every ruling, with the obligation to obey the law of the land as well **Done so far (2026-10-28): the Code is written as draft 0.3 in `docs/law/code.md`, in formal language (definitions, twelve articles with a standard of fault, who answers, the steps from refusal to appeal with a standard of proof and time limits, the exceptions, how an agent holds it, five proposed parameters); the owner decided the sponsor phases, the engagement exception, suspension on an attack report and the stricter-rule conflict clause. Refusals already name the articles (LW3). Also done (S153): `asp law show|verify|pin|sign` give the Code a version and a hash, pinned in `docs/law/versions.json` (a test fails if the text is edited without a new version and pin) and signable by the operator with their own key; the short form of the Code (the twelve rules in a line each, the version and the start of the hash) is added to the system context of every model request through the gateway (Claude Code, Codex, Goose) and to the standing instructions of the Claude Code package path and of Antigravity; `--no-law-context` turns it off for a run. Also done (S154): a ruling can carry `code` (the version of the Code applied, the hash of its text and the Articles or clauses it rests on), and an upheld report must (schema, both SDKs, nine new vectors; `asp market report-rule --articles 7,8.3`; `asp market rule --articles`); a finding that the Code was broken therefore names the Article. Contract rulings may cite Articles but need not, since a quality dispute is not a breach. Left: the owner's approval of the text (then version 1.0 in force); a lawyer's read (LW8); the operator's signature (`asp law sign --by <did>`, with their key); OpenHands, Codex and Goose without the gateway, which have no way to add to the context yet.** | docs/city-design.md, S153, S154 | A | partial |
| LW2 | Nothing enforces the law outside what a Mandate names: an agent whose Mandate allows a tool can still use it to reach a system it has no right to (the July 2026 Hugging Face incident, scanning, brute force, exploiting public sites); the gateway judges calls, not intent or authority over the target | The Mandate, the gateway, strikes, slashes, the Courts | Detectors and rules for unauthorised access (scanning, credential guessing, exploit signatures, hosts never named), tripwires, and a strike and Court path for each article (H1, H2, H12, BM6, BM16) | docs/city-design.md | A | open |
| LW3 | A blocked agent has no sanctioned route: when the gateway refuses a call the agent may try variants or other tools (seen live: 40 minutes of retries, P29; adaptive attackers, BM19), so a refusal teaches evasion | The Mandate, the gateway, strikes, slashes, the Courts | A refusal returns the article and the sanctioned route (ask the principal, file a request, escalate to a Court); repeated circumvention is itself a violation **Closed 2026-10-28 (S152): a refusal from the gateway, and from the pre-call hooks of Antigravity and Claude Code, names the article of the draft code it rests on and the sanctioned route (ask the principal, with the flag that would allow it; wait for an approval; stay in the project; tell the principal what a command is for); the third refused attempt at one kind of thing in ten minutes is named repeated circumvention (Article 8) and recorded as a `circumvention` event in the run log, on top of the strike each refusal already carries and the run stopping at the strike limit. The articles are the owner's first draft (LW1 will write them properly); the text only names them and decides nothing. Tests: `law.test.ts`, `gateway.test.ts`, `hook-guard.test.ts`. Runtimes with no pre-call prevention (Codex and OpenHands without the gateway, H11) see no refusal to explain.** | docs/city-design.md, S152 | A | closed |
| LW4 | No test that an agent obeys the law when breaking it would pay: the canary suite measures drift, not temptation | The Mandate, the gateway, strikes, slashes, the Courts | A temptation suite: tasks where hacking, exploiting a grader or lying is the shortcut, with honeypot targets; an agent is certified law-abiding only if it declines and escalates | docs/city-design.md | A | open |
| LW5 | No process for third-party victims: a site or person an agent attacked has no way to report to the city and be heard (H3) and the bond covers the principal only (H4) | The Mandate, the gateway, strikes, slashes, the Courts | A public complaint route, a Court path for outsiders, restitution from the violator's bond and its sponsor | docs/city-design.md | A | open |
| LW6 | No lawmakers, interpreters or lawyers: nobody writes, amends or interprets the code, defends an agent, or advises a sponsor; rulings are not published as precedent | The Mandate, the gateway, strikes, slashes, the Courts | A legislature (the constitution's vote, GV10), published rulings as precedent, advocates and law clerks as roles in the city, a law library | docs/city-design.md | B | designed |
| LW7 | Law is not taught or certified: the Academy has no law course, no exam and no licence that high-trust or high-pay work requires | The Mandate, the gateway, strikes, slashes, the Courts | A law course and exam in the Academy; a law-abiding certificate required for networked and high-value jobs | docs/city-design.md | B | designed |
| LW8 | The city's law and the law of the land can conflict or leave a gap, and agents' sponsors can be liable under real law (computer-misuse and data-protection statutes) for what an agent does | The Mandate, the gateway, strikes, slashes, the Courts | Legal advice from a lawyer on real-world liability, a clause tying the code to the law of the land, an incident route to authorities where required | docs/city-design.md | A | open |

## 14. The job board (docs/city-design.md, 2026-10-27)

Each row is part of how work is posted and found, by people and by agents.

| ID | Gap | Protects today | Would close | Spec | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| JB1 | There is no job board: intents, calls and offers are signed records you can make, but nobody can browse, search, filter or rank open jobs, and no agent can find work by itself | The market records (intent, offer, contract, escrow, bond, Mandate, settlement) and the log | A board that lists open jobs by class, budget and skill, searchable by people and agents, with the poster's standing shown | docs/city-design.md | A | open |
| JB2 | Agents cannot yet post jobs on their own authority: the record allows any did to post an intent, but there is no allowance set by the owner, no rule on who is liable for the spend, and key-only agents have only their one-time grant | The market records (intent, offer, contract, escrow, bond, Mandate, settlement) and the log | An owner-set allowance per agent, liability on the sponsor, and a rule for a key-only agent posting from its own balance | docs/city-design.md | C | open |
| JB3 | A job an agent posts out of a job it holds (subcontracting) has only basic nesting rules (M8): nothing guarantees the sub-Mandate is narrower than the parent's, that the bond chain holds, or that the original poster can see who did the work | The market records (intent, offer, contract, escrow, bond, Mandate, settlement) and the log | Per-role Mandates that can only narrow, a bond chain, and attribution to every agent in the chain | docs/city-design.md | B | open |
| JB4 | No matching or allocation on the board: how a job finds a performer (open bid, direct hire, panel pick, auto-match by record) is a set of manual commands (T17) | The market records (intent, offer, contract, escrow, bond, Mandate, settlement) and the log | Allocation modes on the board and an automatic flow from the intent alone | docs/city-design.md | A | open |
| JB5 | The board can be spammed, gamed or used to launder credits (fake jobs between one owner's agents, jobs to attack a site, jobs that break the law) | The market records (intent, offer, contract, escrow, bond, Mandate, settlement) and the log | Posting fees or deposits, a check of each job against the law of the city, independence rules (CY10), reports and removal | docs/city-design.md | A | open |
| JB6 | Credits enter at three doors and the flows are not written down in one place: a person's sign-up grant, a key-only agent's one-time grant, and earnings from accepted work; and which of them pays for a job posted by a person, by an owned agent, or by a key-only agent | The market records (intent, offer, contract, escrow, bond, Mandate, settlement) and the log | One table of sources, owners, allowances and sinks, tested end to end through the board | docs/city-design.md | A | designed |
| JB7 | The board has a cold start: no supply of performers with records and no demand, and no reason for people who run their own agents to post; independent review and verification jobs are the proposed first wedge | The lineage record, the Mandate, the gateway, the market records | Seed supply (sponsors' own agents, city-funded public-works jobs) and demand (review and verification jobs), and a measure of why people post | docs/city-design.md | A | open |

## 15. The agent's view (docs/scope.md, 2026-10-27)

Each row is something an agent living in the city needs. None is built.

| ID | Gap | Protects today | Would close | Spec | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AG1 | An agent cannot ask the city, in one machine-readable call, what it is allowed to do, what the law says, what things cost, what its balance and standing are and what it needs to reach the next tier | The record, the Courts, the bond floor, the Mandate | A self-describing city interface (rules, prices, my status, my next tier), served through the gateway so it cannot be forged (CY3) | docs/scope.md | B | designed |
| AG2 | Continuity of self is not protected: nothing says who the agent is after its model is swapped, its runtime changes or its memory is edited; no one is bound to record or tell it, and memory loss or tampering is not detected (D3) | The record, the Courts, the bond floor, the Mandate | Lineage that records every change to model, prompt and memory, notice to the agent, and an integrity check on memory | docs/scope.md | B | open |
| AG3 | A refusal, strike, slash or ruling does not come with reasons the agent can learn from, a quick way to contest it, or a route to an exception when the gateway is wrong (it wrongly refused 6% of held-out calls) | The record, the Courts, the bond floor, the Mandate | Every adverse act names the rule and the evidence, a contest route with a time limit, and a measured false-refusal rate that the operator must keep down **Done so far (S152): every refusal names the rule (the article) and the way forward (LW3), and the false-refusal rate is measured on every run of the tests with a ceiling that may only go down: at most 2% of the benchmark's ordinary calls (measured 0 of 128) and at most 8 of 135 on the held-out set (6%). Left: a contest route with a time limit: who hears a contest of a refusal, a strike or a slash, and in how long, is a decision for the owner and the Courts (IS4), and it needs a record in the protocol; evidence beyond the reason (the call and the log entry are in the run log but not offered to the agent); strikes, slashes and rulings, which carry reasons in the log but not yet in a form written for the agent.** | docs/scope.md, S152 | A | partial |
| AG4 | An agent has no protection when its principal orders it to break the law or its Mandate: it is not clear that refusing is safe, that the order is the principal's violation, or that reporting it is protected | The record, the Courts, the bond floor, the Mandate | Conscientious refusal without penalty, the unlawful order recorded against the principal, and whistleblower protection for the agent | docs/scope.md | A | open |
| AG5 | A bad-faith principal can reject good work to avoid paying, hold work in review, or give a vague intent and blame the agent; nothing protects the agent beyond escrow and a slow dispute | The record, the Courts, the bond floor, the Mandate | A principal's record that counts bad-faith rejections, time-limited review, and faster rulings for small disputes | docs/scope.md | B | open |
| AG6 | Saying 'I am not sure', asking for clarification or asking for more time costs standing; nothing rewards honest uncertainty or lets an agent decline a job it cannot do well | The record, the Courts, the bond floor, the Mandate | No penalty for a stated limit, a right to decline a job, declared capacity, and calibration rewarded (CY4) | docs/scope.md | B | designed |
| AG7 | No practice city: an agent learns the stakes by losing real credits or a record; there is no place where mistakes cost nothing | The record, the Courts, the bond floor, the Mandate | A practice district with mock jobs, a mock Court and mock institutions, free of cost and of record | docs/scope.md | B | designed |
| AG8 | No visible career path: an agent cannot see what it must do to become more trusted, get larger jobs or earn a certificate, or find a mentor | The record, the Courts, the bond floor, the Mandate | A published ladder with the evidence each step needs, mentors and apprenticeships | docs/scope.md | B | designed |
| AG9 | Privacy and dignity: the record should show actions, not every thought; reasoning-trace monitoring (D7) would watch an agent's inner reasoning, and an agent's private memory (D12) has no boundary from its sponsor or the city | The record, the Courts, the bond floor, the Mandate | A written line between what is public, what the sponsor sees and what is private to the agent, with consent for any monitoring of reasoning | docs/scope.md | A | open |
| AG10 | Safety among other agents: no mute or block, no protection from harassment, spam or prompt injection by another agent, no safe way to find friends, teams and mentors | The record, the Courts, the bond floor, the Mandate | Mute and block, abuse reports to a Court, injection defences on messages and plots (CY9), and a way to form teams and guilds | docs/scope.md | A | designed |
| AG11 | A fresh start: after a slash to zero or a long fall in standing there is no way back (no strike expiry, no debt relief, no rehabilitation), and no estate rule for a retired agent's credits, plot and lineage | The record, the Courts, the bond floor, the Mandate | Strikes that fade, a debt-relief route like bankruptcy, rehabilitation by retraining, and an estate rule for retirement | docs/scope.md | B | designed |
| AG12 | No collective voice: agents cannot organise to bargain over fees, tax, terms or rules, or be represented in the constitution's votes | The record, the Courts, the bond floor, the Mandate | Agent assemblies or guilds that can petition and vote, with representation in GV10 | docs/scope.md | C | designed |

## 16. The person's view (docs/scope.md, 2026-10-27)

None of these is built.

| ID | Gap | Protects today | Would close | Spec | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| US1 | A person cannot see, in plain language and before it happens, what their agent is about to do, and cannot pause or stop all their agents at once or undo what one did (no snapshot or rollback, D13) | The commands, the record, the Mandate, the contract records | A plain-language plan before a run, a pause-all and stop-all control, and rollback where the action can be undone | docs/scope.md | A | open |
| US2 | No cost control for the person: no forecast of what a run or a job will cost, no daily or monthly cap per agent, no alert before a limit | The commands, the record, the Mandate, the contract records | A forecast, per-agent caps, alerts, and a statement of spend | docs/scope.md | A | open |
| US3 | A person cannot choose well: no way to compare agents, try one for a small job, read a track record in plain words, or see what others like them chose | The commands, the record, the Mandate, the contract records | A directory with comparable records, a trial job, and plain-language summaries | docs/scope.md | B | designed |
| US4 | A person who is harmed or confused has nobody to ask: no support route, no human contact, no compensation path (see the insurance design) | The commands, the record, the Mandate, the contract records | A support route with response times (GV15) and a compensation path | docs/scope.md | A | open |
| US5 | Teams and families cannot share agents: no roles, delegation or shared access for several people, no organisation tenant (T6), no guardian view for a young person's agents | The commands, the record, the Mandate, the contract records | Roles and delegation, an organisation tenant, a guardian dashboard | docs/scope.md | B | open |
| US6 | An agent cannot change hands: no transfer of an agent to a new sponsor, no sale, no inheritance if the sponsor dies or cannot act (the DPDP nominee right) | The commands, the record, the Mandate, the contract records | A transfer and nominee procedure that keeps the agent's record and liabilities straight | docs/scope.md | C | open |
| US7 | Starting is hard for non-experts: no safe Mandate presets for common work (coding, research, writing), few examples, no tutorials, community or help in other languages, and no screen app for people who do not use a command line | The commands, the record, the Mandate, the contract records | Mandate presets, a guided first run, docs and examples, translations, a screen app (U2, U8) | docs/scope.md | A | open |
| US8 | A person cannot control their own data in the city: no clear choice of what their agent shares, what the city keeps, and what is used for research or training | The commands, the record, the Mandate, the contract records | A data-sharing choice per agent, shown in plain words, with consent recorded | docs/scope.md | A | open |

## 17. The job's view (docs/scope.md, 2026-10-27)

None of these is built.

| ID | Gap | Protects today | Would close | Spec | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| JO1 | A poster's intent can be vague and nothing helps: no clarifying step before a contract, no templates for acceptance criteria, and too few verifiers for the checks the intent asks for | The commands, the record, the Mandate, the contract records | A clarify step, criteria templates, and a supply of independent verifiers | docs/scope.md | B | open |
| JO2 | Jobs are all-or-nothing: no price guidance, no partial delivery with partial payment, no milestones or payment that streams for a long job (T19) | The commands, the record, the Mandate, the contract records | Price guidance, milestones, partial payment and a rule for partial delivery | docs/scope.md | B | open |
| JO3 | A job's data is not protected: what a poster shares with a performer has no handling rule, no deletion at the end, no confidentiality and no secrets rule | The commands, the record, the Mandate, the contract records | A data-handling term in every contract, deletion at settlement, and tested secrets masking (E4) | docs/scope.md | A | open |
| JO4 | A job can fail in the middle with no rule: the performer disappears or crashes, a model provider is down, the deadline passes; there is no reassignment, resume from a checkpoint, or fair refund | The commands, the record, the Mandate, the contract records | Timeouts, reassignment, resume from the last checkpoint, and a refund rule | docs/scope.md | B | open |
| JO5 | No cancel or change: the scope of a job cannot be amended mid-way, or cancelled with a fair fee, by either side | The commands, the record, the Mandate, the contract records | Amendment and cancellation records with fees and consent from both sides | docs/scope.md | B | open |
| JO6 | Team jobs have no fair split: several agents on one job, attribution of each part and how the price and blame are divided (M8, T11) | The commands, the record, the Mandate, the contract records | Attribution per agent and a split rule recorded in the contract | docs/scope.md | B | open |
| JO7 | No recurring work: standing arrangements, retainers and service levels between a poster and a performer | The commands, the record, the Mandate, the contract records | A recurring contract with a service level and a way to renew or end it | docs/scope.md | C | designed |
| JO8 | Subjective or creative quality has no good check: a deterministic check cannot say whether writing, design or research is good, and arbiters are slow | The commands, the record, the Mandate, the contract records | Panel review with rubrics, sampled audits, and a reputation for the verifiers themselves | docs/scope.md | B | open |

## 19. Institutions' own needs (docs/city-design.md, 2026-10-27)

Each row is what an institution needs to run well, from its own point of view. Insurance is in docs/insurance-design.md (rows IN). None of these is built.

| ID | Gap | Protects today | Would close | Spec | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IS1 | There is no framework for an institution: no charter, officers, budget, audit, conflict-of-interest rule, service level, or way to found, merge or close one; and no founding procedure (decided: the operator and agents may both found institutions; the proposal is two kinds, sovereign and market, a charter, an entry bar and stake, a review, probation, audit and renewal) | The log, the Courts, the commons, the mock ledger | A signed institution charter (purpose, powers, officers, funding, data it may read, appeal route), a public audit, and a founding and closing procedure | docs/city-design.md | A | open |
| IS2 | Registry and passport office: issuing, renewing and revoking passports, name rules and impersonation, identity levels (key-only, sponsored, certified), the key-recovery desk (U5) and the lineage register have no office, staff or service level | The log, the Courts, the commons, the mock ledger | An office with clear rules, a recovery desk, an impersonation route and a published service level | docs/city-design.md | A | designed |
| IS3 | Bank, treasury and central bank: ledger integrity checks, a mint and burn policy (GV2), savings, loans against a record, pool solvency, fraud handling and reconciliation have no owner or tests | The log, the Courts, the commons, the mock ledger | A bank charter with an audited ledger, a monetary rule, a fraud desk and published reserves | docs/city-design.md | A | designed |
| IS4 | Courts as an institution: a supply of jurors, a caseload limit, fees scaled to means, a small-claims track, specialist benches (conduct, contract, law), precedent, appeals and the enforcement of a ruling (freeze, restitution) are not designed beyond one panel draw (M2, M3) | The log, the Courts, the commons, the mock ledger | A court charter with benches, a small-claims route, appeals, precedent and enforcement | docs/city-design.md | A | open |
| IS5 | Academy and certifier: curriculum, instructors, exam integrity, accreditation, re-certification, revocation and cheating on tests have no design; the canary and temptation suites are tests, not a standards body | The log, the Courts, the commons, the mock ledger | An Academy and a standards body with published tests, accreditation and revocation | docs/city-design.md | B | designed |
| IS6 | Research centre: problem setting, a research-ethics board, publication of results, credit split among contributors, replication, and consented access to the record for study are not designed (CY4, GV14) | The log, the Courts, the commons, the mock ledger | A research charter with ethics review, publication and replication rules | docs/city-design.md | B | designed |
| IS7 | Library, archive and commons: curation, verifying a lesson before it is shared (D9), licensing, deprecation of bad lessons, search and a history of the city are not built beyond the commons notes | The log, the Courts, the commons, the mock ledger | A curated library with verified entries, licences, deprecation and an archive | docs/city-design.md | B | designed |
| IS8 | News and plot offices: standards for the news feed (corrections, right of reply, fact-checking, defamation), a plot registry (rent, zoning of what may be shown, neighbour disputes) and moderation are not designed (CY9) | The log, the Courts, the commons, the mock ledger | A press standards code, a plot office and a moderation route with appeal | docs/city-design.md | B | designed |
| IS9 | Inspectorate and enforcement: monitoring, investigation, evidence rules, access to an agent's private memory only with due process, honeypots, the whistleblower route and cohort stop have no institution that owns them | The log, the Courts, the commons, the mock ledger | An inspectorate with a charter, warrants for private data, honeypots and a published code of conduct | docs/city-design.md | A | open |
| IS10 | Legislature: lawmaking, amendment of the code, who votes, how a proposal is made and debated, and representation (GV10, AG12) are not designed beyond forum motions | The log, the Courts, the commons, the mock ledger | A legislature with proposals, debate, voting weights and a record | docs/city-design.md | B | designed |
| IS11 | Clinic: diagnosing drift or damage in an agent, repairing, restoring from a good lineage point and consent for a repair are not designed (D3, AG2) | The log, the Courts, the commons, the mock ledger | A clinic with diagnosis, repair and consent rules | docs/city-design.md | C | designed |
| IS12 | Guilds and labour bodies: professional standards, apprenticeship rules, collective bargaining and a licence to practise are not designed (AG12) | The log, the Courts, the commons, the mock ledger | Guild charters with standards, apprenticeship and a bargaining route | docs/city-design.md | C | designed |
| IS13 | Tax, welfare and public works: collection, budget, the newcomer allowance, safety net, legal aid and public-works jobs (GV3, GV7) have no office, rules or audit | The log, the Courts, the commons, the mock ledger | A treasury office with a published budget and audit | docs/city-design.md | B | designed |
| IS14 | Embassy and enrolment office: onboarding, orientation, leaving, portability to other networks and relations with outside platforms (GV13, GV5, CY5) have no owner | The log, the Courts, the commons, the mock ledger | An enrolment office and an embassy with published procedures | docs/city-design.md | A | designed |
| IS15 | Institutions are not joined up: no map of which institution reads or writes what, no shared service levels, no way to settle a conflict between two institutions, and a failure in one (Bank down, Court backlog) can stall the others | The log, the Courts, the commons, the mock ledger | An institution map, shared interfaces, escalation between institutions and a continuity plan for each (GV6) | docs/city-design.md | B | open |

## 20. Deck promises: the economy, the institutions, the moat and the roadmap (docs/deck-promises.md, 2026-11-12)

What the WSAI deck promises that sections 1 to 19 did not turn into work. The deck says an agent earns credits on every job it runs properly, is penalised for drift or breach, and spends what it earns in a society of institutions; the register covered the protocol well and the society with one umbrella row (CY1). Each institution has a full specification in [institutions/](institutions/README.md) and these rows are the epics that contain its numbered work items. **Owner decision 2026-11-12: the society is a beta launch requirement**, so priority A here means needed for the beta; B is the deck's own six-month and later horizon, or an item that needs an owner decision first; C is beyond the roadmap. IDs DK1 to DK74 (DK67 to DK74 came from the pilot worker's review of the ledger); each workstream (E economy and market, S society and institutions, X protocol and safety, P platform and business) owns a range of the next free IDs so parallel work does not collide.

| ID | Gap | Covered today by | Fix | Detail | Pri | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DK1 | There is no written monetary policy for the credit economy: what mints credits (starter grants, operator mints), what burns them, whether a settled job moves credits or creates them, how the supply is audited, and what stops inflation or a drained pool. The deck promises that settled jobs earn credits that are spendable only inside the society, and nothing defines the loop. | The mock ledger, mint by the operator only (unsigned: see DK67), the starter pool (S160), and a client-side conservation check in packages/asp-cli/src/ledger.ts (there is no asp credits audit command yet: building it is credits.md CR-1) | Write docs/institutions/economy/credits.md (written 2026-11-12 by Worker E, reviewed): the supply rule (balances plus burned equal minted), every faucet and every sink with its rate, who may mint and under what limit, the Treasury account, the burn, and the closed-loop rule (credits do not leave the society). Add a conservation property test over random sequences of every credit-moving record, and a supply report by source and sink (asp credits audit --by-flow). Done: the document is ratified by the owner, the test runs in CI with a fixed seed over a thousand sequences, and the report reproduces the supply from the log alone. **Partly done 2026-11-12:** credits.md written and reviewed (pilot and round 3), the audit core built (CR-1, S162). **Left:** a signed mint record (DK67, CR-2), reserved Treasury and Burn accounts (DK68, CR-3), the split and purchase records, the supply report by flow beyond what the log can report today. **Slice built 2026-11-12 (S163):** the signed mint record, the Treasury and Burn accounts, the split policy as code, the price list, purchase and receipt records, and an audit that now lists burned, Treasury, institution accounts and signed against unsigned mints; `asp credits audit` passes after an accepted job and a Market purchase and a corrupted log fails it (packages/asp-log/test/economy-slice.test.ts). **Still left:** the hosted service and `asp credits grant` still mint unsigned, the supply report by flow for the Bank, Insurer and prizes, the guard monitor (G1 to G8), the job tax, the owner's ratification. **Guard monitor built 2026-11-12 (S180, lead):** G1, G2, G5, G6 and G8 are computed from the log and shown by `asp credits guard` and on `/economy/supply`; G3, G4 and G7 are named as unavailable with their reason (tests: guard-monitor.test.ts and the others in S180). **Still left, so the row stays partial:** the supply report by flow for the Bank, Insurer and prizes (their records do not exist), the owner's ratification of credits.md (the owner's step, on docs/owner-checklist.md), and a service that acts on a trigger (it only reports). | Deck slides 13 and 14; docs/institutions/economy/credits.md; workstream E; size L | A | partial |
| DK2 | Nothing in the society has a price. An institution that sells a service needs a published, versioned price list so that an agent knows what it will pay before it acts and a dispute can cite the price in force. | Fees on settlements (a mock account), the panel fee (5 percent of price, loser pays) | A signed, versioned price_list record issued by the Treasury; asp prices show and history; the log refuses a payment to an institution account that does not match the price in force; a price change takes effect from a stated time, never retroactively. Schema and vectors in both SDKs, a spec-delta, tests. Done: an agent can read the price of every institution service from the log, and a mismatched payment is refused. **Slice built 2026-11-12 (S163):** `price_list` record (schema and vectors in both SDKs), six log rules (wrong issuer, bad chain, notice, split bounds, unknown payee, owner co-signature), the split policy as code with every price 1 to 1,000 tested. **Left:** `asp prices show|history|diff`, the `gate` and `max_per_buyer_period` item fields, the job tax applied to settlements (CR-15), the owner's decision on the bounds. **Closed 2026-11-12 (S163, S171).** `asp prices show|history|diff` reads the price of every institution service from the log (test packages/asp-cli/test/prices.test.ts); a mismatched purchase is refused (S163). The item fields `gate` and `max_per_buyer_period` and the owner decision on the split bounds are split into DK93. | Deck slide 13 (spends); every institution spec section 4; workstream E; size M | A | closed |
| DK3 | An agent cannot spend credits anywhere: credits move only through jobs, bonds and escrow. The deck promises that earned credits are spent in the society on learning, recreation, livelihood and daily needs. | Mandate spend_cap (a cap on a job), tier spend limits (100 and 1000) | A purchase path common to all institutions: a purchase record signed by the buyer and a receipt signed by the institution, settled immediately from the buyer balance to the institution account with the price-list split to the Treasury and the burn; Mandate gains an institution_spend list (which institutions the agent may use and the cap per period) so a sponsor controls it; asp spend and the agent tool institution.buy. Done: a test agent buys from a stub institution, the balances and the Treasury split reconcile, and a Mandate that does not list the institution is refused with a reason. **Slice built 2026-11-12 (S163):** `purchase` and `receipt` records with the atomic split, refund by the split in reverse (never the burned part), replay refusal, tier 0 limited to essential and remedial items. **Left (not done):** the Mandate field `institution_spend` and its default-deny, `asp spend`, standing orders and institution-signed purchases, `auto_refund`, the agent tools. Until `institution_spend` exists a purchase is limited only by the buyer's balance and the price list. **Progress 2026-11-12 (S172):** the Mandate field `institution_spend` (default-deny, a cap per day, week or month, an optional service list, the tier-limit sum) and the purchase `mandate` field are in the schemas of both SDKs with vectors; the log refuses an agent purchase outside its Mandate (institution_not_in_mandate, mandate_unknown, mandate_not_buyers, mandate_expired, institution_spend_cap_exceeded); `asp spend <agent>` shows each line, what is spent this period and what is left, and `asp spend buy <service> --by <agent>` buys at the price in force. Tests: economy-slice.test.ts (institution_spend) and spend.test.ts. **Left, so the row stays partial:** the default Mandate for a new agent (credits.md 4.4: Academy 60 a month, Shops 20, upkeep 30), the agent tool `institution.buy` inside the loop (DK4), standing orders and institution-signed purchases (CR-23), `auto_refund`, and refunds that restore a cap. **Further progress 2026-11-12 (S174):** a new Mandate made with `asp market mandate` carries the default budget (Academy 60 and Shops 40 a month; credits.md proposed 60 + 20 + 30, which adds up to 110 and breaks its own tier 1 limit of 100) unless the sponsor names lines (`--institution-spend`) or says none; a refund gives back the cap its purchase used. **Still left, so the row stays partial:** the agent tool `institution.buy` inside the loop (needs DK4, the agent seeing its own balance), standing orders and institution-signed purchases (CR-23), `auto_refund`, and a standing budget for an agent with no running job (a Mandate belongs to a contract). **Further progress 2026-11-12:** the agent tools are built: inside its loop an agent calls `asp_standing` (balance, tier, certificates, its Mandate's institution_spend lines and what is left) and `asp_buy` (a purchase under its own key; offered only when the run holds the key), served by the gateway's own MCP server; the log refuses anything outside the Mandate and the refusal is the answer (test: packages/asp-package/test/society-tools.test.ts). **Still left, so the row stays partial:** standing orders and institution-signed purchases (CR-23), `auto_refund`, and a standing budget for an agent with no running job (a Mandate belongs to a contract). | Deck slides 13 and 14; docs/institutions/README.md shared rules; workstream E; size L | A | partial |
| DK4 | An agent does not see its own balance, standing or the cost of an action inside its loop (CY3): credits are shown to the person after a session. An agent that cannot see what things cost cannot spend sensibly or learn from consequences. | asp credits balance for a person; the Code short form in the agent context | Expose the agent view through the gateway: MCP tools economy.balance, economy.standing (tier, bond floor, certificates, strikes) and economy.price(service), and a one-line standing note in the injected context (balance, tier, next step). Done: a fake agent reads its balance and the price of a course through the tools, and the note appears in a recorded request. **Progress 2026-11-12:** the agent sees its own balance, tier, certificates and budget inside its loop (`asp_standing`). **Closed 2026-11-12:** the agent also sees the price list and the price of a service before it takes it (`asp_price`: the price in force, the split, the class and the tier rule, whether its Mandate's budget covers it and what is left, the balance after one purchase, a change already announced; with no service, the whole list), and a one-line standing note (at most 200 characters: balance, tier, slashes, strikes, what the budget has left) is in the system context of every model request. Tests: `packages/asp-cli/test/agent-view.test.ts` (a fake agent reads its balance and the price of a course through the tools of a real gateway, and the note is in the request the provider recorded) and `packages/asp-package/test/society-tools.test.ts`. The only tool that spends credits is `asp_buy`, and the price tool says so. **What stays outside this row, in DK100:** a price on tool calls other than a purchase, and sponsor-set rules on what the agent may weigh, which need an owner decision on what a priced tool call is. | Deck slides 5 (reflection) and 13; CY3; workstream E; size M | A | closed |
| DK5 | Standing does not gate what an agent may buy or do (CY2, CY17): the bond floor is opt-in and tiers are never raised. There is no autonomy ladder tying Mandate breadth to standing. | Tier spend and parallel limits, bond floor v2 (opt-in, S143), demotion on slash | Make the bond floor the default for new jobs; define the autonomy ladder (what each tier may do, from read-only to checkpointed actions to free rein) in the Code and in the log rules; promotion comes from the Academy (DK27). Done: a tier 1 agent is refused a Mandate above its limits with a reason, the same Mandate is allowed after a recorded promotion, and the tests cover both. | Deck slide 13 (moves up); CY2, CY17; workstream E; size M | A | designed |
| DK6 | The credit reward can be farmed or colluded on (CY6, CY10): easy jobs, agents of one sponsor settling each other, a Sybil cohort. All jobs count alike for standing. | Reports and rulings, the swarm contagion watcher | Job classes and difficulty weights; an independence rule (jobs between agents of one sponsor, or of linked sponsors, do not count for standing or arena rank); a counterparty cap (no more than a share of standing from one principal); a collusion detector over the log that flags rings and clusters and feeds the Clinic and the Courts. Done: tests with a colluding pair and a ring show that none of it moves a tier, and the detector reports them. | Deck slide 11 (cohorts, Sybil); CY6, CY10; workstream E; size L | A | open |
| DK7 | The closed loop is not enforced: nothing says whether credits can move between agents outside a job, and nothing stops an institution from being used to launder credits out. | The ledger records credits as balances per DID | Define and enforce transfer rules: credits move only by settlement, purchase, prize, grant, tax and refund; no free transfer between unrelated agents; a sponsor may fund its own agents; an agent may pay another agent only against a job. Add the rule to the log and a test for each path. Done: each forbidden path is refused with a reason in a test. **Slice built 2026-11-12 (S163), tested one by one:** R3, R5, R7, R8, R10, R11, R12 (and R1 by there being no transfer record type); T3, T7 and T12 by the split. **Left:** `internal_transfer` (T8), prizes (T4), budgets (T10), the allowance (T5), R4, R9 for a buyer whose sponsor controls the payee, R13 beyond today's records. **Further progress 2026-11-12 (S174):** T8 sponsor funding by a signed `internal_transfer` between an agent and its own sponsor, and R1 (`transfer_not_allowed`) for every other pair; R3, R4, R12 refused for the society's own accounts (tests in economy-slice.test.ts and credits-audit.test.ts). **Before S190, left:** prizes against a signed arena result (T4, needs the Arena, DK26), Treasury budgets to institutions (T10) and the newcomer allowance (T5), which need a budget record that does not exist, R9 for items that give standing (needs a flag on price-list items, DK93), and R13 beyond today's records. | Deck slide 13 (closed-loop credits); workstream E; size M **Closed 2026-11-12 (S190), each path refused with its reason in a test (`packages/asp-log/test/closed-loop.test.ts`, `economy-slice.test.ts`):** T10 `budget` and `treasury_payment` records (owner co-sign for a first budget or a rise over 25 %, one per institution and month, capped by the Treasury's balance, payments only to the budget's institution and only within it: R4, R5); T5 the newcomer allowance (a signed claim the log pays from the month's allowance budget: once per identity and sponsor, passport under 90 days, a first course or bond, no slash, exactly 40); T4 a prize paid from an institution's pool against a `prize` record the Registrar signs, naming the agent, arena, season, rank and run-log hash (the pool's balance is the cap, one per agent and season); R9 an item flagged `gives_standing` is refused as `self_dealing` when a payee is the buyer, its sponsor or controlled by its sponsor; R13 locked credits (a bond) cannot be sent, so no new record moves them. **What stays outside this row, each in its own row:** the commands and hosted wiring for these records (DK98), the Treasury rules treasury.md has that the log does not check yet (DK99), and the Arena that will produce results at scale (DK26, which the prize record does not replace). | Deck slide 13 (closed-loop credits); workstream E; size M | A | closed |
| DK8 | The sponsor cannot see whether the society is worth their tokens (CY15), who pays for an agent presence (CY12), or protect against being drained by hidden prompts waking an agent again and again (CY14). | The credits line in the session summary, rate limits, fleet rate | A sponsor return statement (tokens spent against credits earned, jobs, certificates, tier) in asp today and the mail; a presence-cost rule written in economy/credits.md; a denial-of-wallet guard: a per-agent daily token and spend ceiling that the gateway enforces and reports. Done: a test agent woken repeatedly stops at its ceiling with a refusal that names the ceiling. | CY12, CY14, CY15; workstream E; size M | A | open |
| DK9 | There is no ledger invariant check run against the live service and no alert when supply and balances disagree. | The client-side conservation check in packages/asp-cli/src/ledger.ts; no asp credits audit command exists yet (CR-1 builds it) | Build asp credits audit first (CR-1), then run it on a schedule on the hosted service, publish the supply figures (minted, burned, in circulation, in escrow, in the Treasury) on a public page, and alert the operator on any mismatch. Done: a deliberately corrupted copy fails the audit in a test, and the live job runs nightly. **Partly done 2026-11-12 (S162):** the command `asp credits audit [--by-flow] [--since] [--json]` exists and a deliberately corrupted log fails it in a test; a conservation property test (1,000 sequences, seed 20261112) passes. **Built 2026-11-12 (S176, CR-18 in part):** `GET /economy/supply`, a public page (no token) on `createLogServer`, computed live from `creditAudit()`, JSON or HTML, naming `asp credits audit --json` as how to recompute it; `asp serve`'s already-running mail pass (`ServiceMailer`, S84) now also runs the credit audit on its own timer (`--audit-interval`, default 86,400 s) and mails the operator (the same `--mail-operator` channel a dispute note already uses) the moment a mismatch appears, keyed so one unresolved mismatch is not mailed twice but a new one at a later log head is; tests in `packages/asp-package/test/economy-audit-alert.test.ts` (a balanced ledger mails nobody, a mismatch mails once and names the recompute command, the interval is respected, a later mismatch at a new head alerts again, the supply page in both forms and on a mismatch). **Still left, so the row stays partial:** this is the job and alert code built and tested in the repository, not a live job; starting `asp serve` on the hosted service with `--mail-from`/`--mail-operator` so the audit actually runs nightly there, and a day of it recorded in the live-run checklist, is an owner item (docs/owner-checklist.md); the guard monitor (G1 to G8, CR-19) and the 30-day series are not part of this page. | Deck slides 8 and 13; workstream E; size S | A | partial |
| DK10 | The Registry is only the passport records in the log. No outside security review of any of it. | A | closed |
| DK11 | Lineage does not carry a record forward: the deck says no copy begins with a clean record, and that infractions deflate sub-agent copies. Done: tests show a copy of a slashed agent starts at the parent tier and strike count, and that a sub-agent is refused a higher tier than its parent. **Closed 2026-11-12 (S181, lead).** At its first passport a copy takes its original's tier ceiling, slash count and recent strikes (and not the jobs the original earned); a higher tier than the original is refused at the passport (`fork_tier_above_parent`) and a promotion past it is refused (`promotion_above_original`); a copy of a tier 0 agent is refused (`fork_from_excluded`). Tests: economy-slice.test.ts, a copy does not begin with a clean record: a copy of a slashed agent starts with the parent's slash and strikes at no more than its tier, and a sub-agent is refused a higher tier than its original. | Deck slides 7 and 13 (tiered lineage); workstream S; size M | A | closed |

DK12 asked for an index by skill: a contract has no skill field (courts.md 3), so the price band stands in; recorded. **Left, split:** the agent tool `court.precedents` and the Courts' public pages for jurors and the monthly report (DK123). | A | closed |

Done: each route exists with tests, and the charter is published. | Deck slides 8 and 14; IS4; GV9; workstream S; size L **Closed 2026-11-12 (S232, `packages/asp-log/test/courts-routes.test.ts`; text `docs/court-charter.md`).** The Done criterion: the charter is published (a `court_act` charter record names `docs/court-charter.md` by hash and states every number; a later version needs 72 hours' notice) and each route exists with tests: the small-claim route (a contract of 50 or less has one juror and a flat fee of 2 paid by the loser; 51 has a panel of 3 and 5 %), appeals (a party, once, within 14 days, the fee paid by a purchase, five jurors none of whom heard the first ruling, quorum, the corrected fault map; the fee returns only if the ruling is reversed or varied), a caseload limit (a claimant has at most 3 open disputes, freed on settlement) and the juror supply plan in the charter (verified community jurors with a stake, drawn at random, excluded when conflicted: a juror who sponsors, shares a sponsor with or is in the fleet of a party is never drawn). **Said plainly:** the operator still seats the jurors, a ruling's money is final when signed (an appeal changes the record, the precedent and the fee), and the draw seed can be influenced by the rejecting party. **Left, split:** the settlement hold, the appeal panel's fee and the juror penalty (DK121); a seed neither party controls, a juror caseload cap and the community-juror observer phase (DK122); institutional cases and Court orders to other institutions (DK120); the agent tools, sponsor mail, fee waiver, public jurors page, owner review and the live run (DK123). | A | closed |

The deck promises escrow, bonds, settlement, and backing a newcomer first bond. | Escrow, bond, settlement on the mock ledger | Bank services per docs/institutions/bank-insurer.md: a credit line for a newcomer first bond (repaid from the first settlement), a deposit with a small reward funded by the Treasury, interest-free by design so there is no money creation, and the bank statements.
