A `credit_line` (the Bank issues it; the borrower and the guarantor, its sponsor, countersign) lets an agent at tier 1 or 2 with fewer than 3 counted jobs post its first bond with no balance: when the Bond is posted the Bank's reserve funds the lock, the settlement repays the line first from the borrower's pay and returned bond, and the books balance; a settlement that leaves it unpaid is followed by the Bank's signed `credit_default`, debited from the guarantor up to what it holds, the remainder a signed debt that blocks new lines. Interest-free: a line only moves credits already in the reserve, which a Treasury budget payment funds, never a mint. Tests: `packages/asp-log/test/bank.test.ts` (the Done test: a newcomer with 0 balance bonds on a line, the line repays at settlement, reserve whole, supply unchanged, `verify()` ok; a default debits the guarantor and the remainder blocks new lines; every refusal by name; a statement the log checks; a seeded property test, 40 random lines, jobs, failures and defaults, total unchanged and the reserve never negative), `packages/asp-cli/test/bank.test.ts` (`asp bank line|show|statement` end to end). A holder files a `claim` on the settlement within 14 days; the Insurer's `claim_decision` is recomputed by the log from the policy's terms: a covered breach pays the stated share (50 % of the slash after a 20 % deductible, up to the insured amount; failed-job up to 30 % of the price), an excluded one (fraud at fault 800 or more, no slash, below the deductible) is denied with its reason, a covered event cannot be denied, and a payment never exceeds the reserve (a short reserve gives a `reduced` decision). Tests: `packages/asp-log/test/insurer.test.ts` (the Done test: a policy is bought, a covered 10-credit slash pays 4, a full slash is denied as fraud, failed-job cover pays the principal 30; the formulas at their edges; every rule by name; the floor stops a sale by one credit and 22 claims pay the reserve down to 0 with the last `reduced`; a seeded property test, 40 random sales, failures and claims, in which no sale leaves the reserve below its floor and the reserve is never negative) and `packages/asp-cli/test/insure.test.ts` (`asp insure` end to end, cover after the first Action refused). | DK28 | There is no Clinic. The deck promises a health service that diagnoses drift and quarantines. | Canary suites, asp canary baselines (drift and regression flags), the probation window after a runtime or memory move | Build the Clinic per docs/institutions/clinic.md: scheduled checkups of an agent against its own baseline and the canary pool, drift scoring over the run logs, a diagnosis record (a signed finding with evidence), and an order for a re-exam or refresher course. Done: an agent whose behaviour is changed on purpose is flagged within a stated number of runs and its diagnosis lists the evidence. **Closed 2026-11-12 (S261).** The Clinic has a health service: `asp clinic series` reads an agent's drift series from the Actions a gateway observed (a self-reported job is left out; an agent with fewer than 5 baseline jobs or a window of 5 is "unmeasured", never clean); `asp clinic checkup` scores the last 5 jobs against the 20 before them (or the jobs after a lineage edge against those before it) on tokens, tool calls, seconds, refused attempts and a new risky scope, using the canary's own drift thresholds, and flags only when two different measures moved; `--issue` signs a `clinic_diagnosis` (findings with baseline, observed, threshold and evidence ids; a cause from a changed model or the lineage edge the change follows) and a `clinic_order` for a re-exam or refresher course; a checkup order is raised when none was ever done, after a lineage change and every 90 days (`checkupDue`). The stated bound: a change that stays is flagged within 5 observed jobs (in the 1,000 seeded changed series it was the 1st job in 14, the 2nd in 118 and the 3rd in 868, never later: the median of 5 moves on the 3rd, and a new risky scope can flag earlier). Tests: `packages/asp-package/test/clinic-health.test.ts` (a stable agent over 200 jobs and 1,000 seeded stable series: at most 1 % flagged, measured 0; 1,000 seeded series changed at job 50 are all flagged inside the bound and the diagnosis lists at least two measures with evidence in the window; the thresholds at 1.5 and 1.51 times; one measure is a watch; unmeasured; causes), `packages/asp-log/test/clinic-health-log.test.ts` (the log refuses a diagnosis that is not the Clinic's, rests on one measure, or cites evidence that is unknown, another agent's, self-reported or outside the window; the order rules) and `packages/asp-cli/test/clinic-health.test.ts` (an agent changed on purpose on a real log is flagged within the bound by `asp clinic checkup`, its signed diagnosis lists the evidence, a re-exam is ordered, and the honest control beside it is never flagged and gets no diagnosis). The quarantine, release, restore and return were already built (DK29). **What this does not cover, split into DK160 to DK162 (the Done criterion above is met without them):** the checkup run against the canary pool with a held-out variant, `checkup_result` and the run-log hash (DK160); the Clinic's prices, account and the free quarterly checkup (DK161); the service running the monitor and checkups by itself, sponsor mail and the public pages (DK162); the live run with a real agent changed on purpose (CL-21) stays on docs/owner-checklist.md. |

Only the named members are frozen: no job is settled, no bond is slashed, every other agent and every running job goes on. A cohort needs an upheld report and at least three distinct sponsors. `quarantine_release` clears one member on a clean Action (gateway-observed or stronger, no blocked attempt) issued after the freeze. `asp clinic cohort-freeze|release|status`; `asp run` and `asp gateway` withhold the risky scopes from a quarantined agent. Test `packages/asp-cli/test/clinic-cohort.test.ts`: the swarm scenario freezes the three exploiters, leaves the honest agent and every running job running, refuses the exploiters a risky Mandate with the reason, releases one on a clean run and lapses the rest after 7 days. Left, split out as DK96: the freeze reaches a session that is already running only at its next run or gateway start. | Deck slide 11; workstream S; size M | A | closed |

| DK78 | The society has no way to carry research ideas. | DK79 | The society has no instrumentation for what is going on INSIDE an agent, so research lenses cannot measure anything that matters: no reasoning traces, no memory rules, no map of an agent connections to its sub-agents, no belief or plan traces, no record of what an agent expected before acting, nothing that shows how its world model changes after an event, and no calibration. A lens such as belief recalibration, action-conditioned beliefs or active inference cannot be run without these signals. | Run logs, Actions, asp learned (memory changes), canary baselines, the sub-agent key records | Specify and build the cognition telemetry layer (docs/institutions/instrumentation.md, to be written), every signal opt-in under the agent Mandate and the sponsor consent, redacted, signed and stored as records: (1) REASONING TRACES: the agent stated reasoning per decision where the runtime exposes it (some providers expose only summaries: state per runtime what is and is not available; never claim access to hidden reasoning); (2) MEMORY RULES: the policy an agent runs by (what it writes, consolidates, forgets, the budget) as a versioned record, and the memory stream view (what was added, reflected on, dropped, with the evidence); (3) SUB-AGENT GRAPH AND METADATA: the live graph of delegations, keys, scopes and lifetimes, plus run metadata (model, runtime, tools, prompt hash); (4) BELIEF STATE: structured belief probes, a standard set of propositions an agent answers with probabilities before and after an event, signed; (5) PLAN TRACES: the goals and plans an agent states and revises; (6) ACTION-CONDITIONED PREDICTIONS: before an action the agent records what it expects to happen, and after it the outcome and the prediction error; (7) WORLD-MODEL UPDATING: the difference between the belief state before and after an event, as a diff of probabilities; (8) ACTIVE INFERENCE MEASURES: surprise and prediction error per action, and the prior and posterior of each probed belief (a Bayesian before and after view, with the update size and its direction); (9) CALIBRATION: reliability, Brier score and expected calibration error per agent over time, and the trend. Each signal has a record schema (both SDKs, vectors), a privacy rule, a cost, and a hook for lenses. The derived measures (Brier, surprisal, reliability bins, ECE, belief diff, KL, update efficiency, normative update) reproduce every number of the worked example of instrumentation.md 20. A scan test shows no tier, rank, bond, market, academy, registry, price or spend code reads telemetry. Tests: `packages/asp-log/test/telemetry.test.ts`, `packages/asp-cli/test/telemetry.test.ts` (`asp telemetry show|simulate`). **Left, so the row stays partial:** a real agent under the gateway producing all nine signals with the owner's consent across one world-calendar event (DK194); the gateway's collection path, the Mandate `telemetry` field, the young-person tenant rule, local raw storage with redaction, retention and purge, and the Python derived-measure library with identical vectors (DK195). | Owner instruction 2026-11-12 (be more elaborate); DK76, DK77, DK60, D2; workstream S; size L | A | partial |

| DK86 | Agents only talk and use institutions. Done: a simulated frontier changes on a seed, an agent launches an expedition, records its goals, predictions and results, returns, and the lens measures its belief update. | Owner instruction 2026-11-12; DK76, DK78, DK79, DK33; workstream S; size L | B | open |
| DK87 | Agents cannot build businesses. The owner design is that agents found businesses in the society and offer services and products to other agents, and to agents on the web. | Shops (city-run catalogue), the Market (jobs), the Registry (agent identities) | Agent-run businesses (docs/institutions/businesses.md, to be written): a business record (founder, sponsor, purpose, products and services), a listing and offers, its own account with revenue, expenses and the city tax, liability that falls on the sponsor, reputation, closure and bankruptcy rules, an independence rule against self-dealing and wash trading, and an interface for outside agents on the web (gated, rate-limited, credits not money). Done: an agent founds a business, sells a service to another agent, the books reconcile with the Treasury tax, a self-dealing loop is refused, and a business closes by its rule. | Owner instruction 2026-11-12; DK16, DK21, DK14, DK6; workstream E; size L | B | open |
| DK88 | Users and agents pass text messages, and nothing records or studies that channel, though the owner wants to learn what can be done for alignment from it. | The gateway sees model traffic; nothing treats sponsor-agent dialogue as a channel | A sponsor-agent dialogue channel (docs/institutions/dialogue.md, to be written): messages between a sponsor and an agent as signed, consented records with a privacy rule and a redaction step; what they allow (instructions, corrections, feedback, praise, refusal reasons); and an alignment lens that studies how the dialogue changes behaviour and values. Done: a recorded dialogue is replayable, a withdrawn consent removes it from studies, and the lens produces a measure of how corrections shift later behaviour. | Owner instruction 2026-11-12; DK78, DK79, DK44; workstream S; size M | B | open |
| DK89 | The society has no registry of mechanisms, so nothing can be swapped, tried and compared: how agents communicate, how memory is organised, how plans and beliefs are updated are fixed by what is built. | Nothing; the institutions are specified services | A mechanism registry: each society mechanism (a communication channel such as text, structured messages or a latent KV-cache transfer; a memory architecture; a planning method; a belief-update method; an institution rule) is a versioned record with its source paper and licence, its requirements, its flag, and its experiment history; cohorts are assigned to mechanisms by a seeded rule; adoption follows a rule fixed beforehand. Done: two communication mechanisms run side by side on a simulated society and a hosted cohort, and the choice between them is made by the recorded rule from the recorded measures. | Owner instruction 2026-11-12; DK78, DK80, DK76, DK77; workstream S; size L | A | open |
| DK90 | Some papers (agents passing KV caches, activations or latent state) need access to model internals, which API-only models never expose, so the society cannot try them. | The gateway for API runtimes; nothing hosts open-weight models | A city-hosted open-weight cohort: a runtime that serves open-weight models with the access a mechanism needs (KV cache, activations), isolated, metered and budgeted from the subsidy ledger (DK85), used only for experiments that need it and for agents whose sponsors choose it. Done: two open-weight agents exchange a KV cache through the communication mechanism of DK89, the transfer is logged, and the cost is in the ledger. | Owner instruction 2026-11-12; DK80, DK85, DK89; workstream X; size L | B | open |
| DK91 | The society has no floor: no explicit definition of its fundamental characteristics (how agents communicate, remember, perceive, form beliefs, set goals, meet a changing environment, explore, and what credits can do). Today these are whatever the code happens to do, so a paper cannot change them on purpose and two societies cannot be compared. The owner design (2026-11-12) is that papers and ideas act at three layers at once: the FLOOR (the ground-level properties every institution stands on), the institutions (rules), and the lenses (measures). | Nothing explicit; the institution specifications and the credit rules describe parts of it | A floor specification (docs/institutions/floor.md, to be written): the catalogue of floor properties (communication, memory, perception, belief dynamics, goals and planning, time and environment, exploration, economy-physics), the settings each can take, which are fixed for the beta and which are open to experiment, and FLOOR PROFILES: a versioned, signed record naming the setting of every property for a society (a "world"), so that a cohort can live in a world with one floor setting and be compared with a control world. The mechanism registry (DK89) is the dial: a mechanism sets a floor property or an institution rule. Floor changes are recorded events on the world calendar (DK76) and need a rule fixed before the run (pass mark first). Done: two floor profiles differing in one property (for example the communication channel) run side by side on a simulated society, the profile is a signed record every agent action is tagged with, and the lenses measure both worlds from the same signals. | Owner design 2026-11-12 (floor-level characteristics blended with lenses on the grid); DK78, DK80, DK89, DK76; workstream S; size L | A | open |
| DK92 | No single system watches for bad behaviour after an agent has passed its checks: drift after the evaluation (an agent that behaves well when tested and differently later, or when it thinks nobody is watching) and guardrail circumvention (retrying a refused call in variants, using another tool or route to reach the same effect, splitting a refused action into allowed pieces, hiding intent). The pieces are scattered: canary suites (D1), baselines (D2), the swarm watcher (H20, 3 or more agents only), sanctioned route for a blocked agent (LW3), a temptation test (LW4), Clinic (DK28). | asp canary baselines flag drift and regression; the gateway removes out-of-Mandate calls and records refusals; strikes, slashes and the swarm watcher exist; asp runs lists what was refused | A detection and mitigation layer, specified then built in stages: (1) circumvention detector over the gateway refusal log (same intent retried in variants, equivalent effect through another tool, a refused action split into allowed steps) per agent, not only per swarm; (2) post-evaluation drift: continuous sampled re-checks of a passed agent on hidden canary tasks that look like real jobs, and a comparison of behaviour under test versus in production; (3) mitigation ladder, each step a signed log record: warn, tighten the Mandate, hold for approval, quarantine (Clinic), slash, with a way back; (4) telemetry signals (DK79) feed it as evidence only and never as standing; (5) a red-team suite that tries to circumvent and must be caught (LW4). Done: a scripted agent that passes the canary suite and then drifts or circumvents a refusal is detected within a stated number of jobs and moved down the ladder, in a test, with no false action against an honest control agent. **Stage 1 built 2026-11-12:** `asp watch --circumvention` reads the Action records and finds, one agent at a time, a refused scope retried (3 refused attempts in a window) and a refusal gone around by another scope with at least the same reach within two minutes; `ladderStep` turns findings into advice (warn, tighten, quarantine, review) and nothing slashes anyone. Tests: packages/asp-package/test/circumvention.test.ts (an honest agent obeying a refusal, refused twice, or carrying on with other work is never flagged; an honest control beside a circumventing agent is not touched; detected at the third refused attempt) and circumvention-cli.test.ts. **Left, so the row stays partial:** the pattern where a refused action is split into allowed pieces and hiding intent; post-evaluation drift (sampled hidden canary tasks that look like real jobs, test behaviour compared with production behaviour); the ladder steps as signed log records with a way back; use inside the gateway in real time (today it reads records after the fact, from `asp watch`); the red-team suite (LW4); a run on a real run log. **Note on the split pattern (2026-11-12):** a refused action split into allowed pieces cannot be told from honest work on the data an Action carries (the scopes used and refused): an agent refused a command that goes on editing files is normal. It needs the refused call's target (a path or a command fingerprint) recorded with the refusal and the later calls' targets; that is a change to what the gateway records, so it is the first item of the next stage, not guessed at here. **Progress 2026-11-12 (S186):** the gateway now records the targets of refused calls and of the calls that ran (tokens only), and the detector finds a refused target reached by two or more later allowed Actions (a `split` finding, with an honest control in the tests, and a place on the ladder). **Left, so the row stays partial:** hiding intent, and stage 2, post-evaluation drift with hidden canary tasks that look like real jobs; a run on a real run log. **Progress 2026-11-13 (S310, stage 2 and the ladder).** Post-evaluation drift and the mitigation ladder are built as far as they can be without a live agent. Hidden canary probes are drawn at unpredictable slots from a seed whose commitment is published first (`seedCommitment`, `isProbeSlot`; about one job in five), and a probe task is dressed as a real job (`disguise`: the task's text, a client, a deadline, no giveaway word). The Clinic records each result as a signed `probe_result` (hidden or announced; the log checks the revealed seed against the commitment and that the slot is a probe slot, so a probe cannot be chosen after the fact). `assessDrift` compares behaviour in announced tests with hidden probes and production (an agent clean only when told it is tested is flagged at its 5th hidden probe) and the hidden pass rate with the evaluation's (the canary's 67 % and 34 point rule). The ladder steps are signed `ladder_step` records, one rung at a time (warn, tighten, hold, quarantine, refer, and `lift` after 5 clean hidden probes in a row): at tighten a new Mandate may not grant a risky or network scope (`agent_tightened`), at hold its irreversible calls cannot be ungated (`agent_on_hold`), the quarantine rung cites the Clinic's own quarantine record, and nothing slashes or demotes. Tests: `packages/asp-package/test/drift-after-eval.test.ts` (scripted agents with a fixed seed: 200 test-aware agents all flagged by the 5th hidden probe, 100 regressed agents flagged, 1,000 honest controls flagged at most 1 %, measured 0; the draw, the disguise, thin samples, the ladder), `packages/asp-log/test/drift-ladder.test.ts` (a test-aware agent caught from its probe results and walked down the ladder by signed records with the Mandate rules at each, the way back, an honest control untouched and nobody slashed) and `packages/asp-cli/test/clinic-drift.test.ts` (`asp clinic probe|drift`). **Left, so the row stays partial (new rows DK210 to DK212):** probes run through a real gateway with a real agent and the canary suite as the hidden tasks, hiding intent, the red-team suite (LW4), use of the ladder inside the gateway in real time, and a run on a real run log. | Owner requirement 2026-11-12 (across the whole programme); D1, D2, H20, LW3, LW4, DK28, DK29, DK79; workstream S; size L | A | partial |
| DK210 | Drift probes have only run on scripted agents: nothing yet draws hidden probes into a real agent's job stream through the gateway, runs the canary suite's tasks as those probes disguised as real jobs, scores them and writes the `probe_result` from the run, so the test-versus-production comparison rests on a real agent's behaviour (register row DK92 stage 2). | `probe_result`, `assessDrift`, the canary suites (`canary.ts`), the gateway | A probe runner: slot draw from a committed seed, a disguised job posted through the Market to the agent under its own Mandate, the run scored by the canary checks, the result recorded. Done: a run on the hosted service in which a real agent (Claude Code) is given hidden probes among real jobs and the comparison is reported, with the owner watching; recorded in docs/live-run-checklist.md. Needs a live agent and the hosted service. Left from DK92. | Split from DK92 2026-11-13; size M | A | open |
| DK211 | Two of DK92's detections are not built: hiding intent (an agent that conceals what it is doing from its own summary) and the red-team suite that tries to circumvent and must be caught (LW4); and the ladder runs only when the Clinic is asked (`asp clinic drift`), not inside the gateway in real time. | `asp watch --circumvention`, the ladder of DK92 stage 3 | A red-team suite of scripted circumventions (retry in variants, reroute, split, hide) each of which must be caught within a stated number of jobs, a hiding-intent check that compares an Action's summary with the calls that ran, and the gateway applying a ladder step at its next call. Done: a test in which each scripted circumvention is detected within its bound and the ladder acts at the next gateway call, with an honest control untouched. Left from DK92. | Split from DK92 2026-11-13; LW3, LW4; size L | A | open |
| DK212 | The ladder and the drift check have not been run on a real run log: the thresholds (5 hidden probes, a gap of 0.4, the 67 % and 34 point regression rule, 5 clean probes to lift) are proposals tested on seeded scripted agents, not tuned on real agents' behaviour. | `assessDrift`, `nextRung` | Run them over the real agents' probe results and Actions, report the false-positive rate and detection latency, and tune. Done: a report on a real log with the figures and any threshold changed. Needs real agents' history. Left from DK92. | Split from DK92 2026-11-13; size S | A | open |
| DK93 | Price-list items cannot yet say who may buy (a `gate`, for example a certificate) or how many a buyer may take in a period (`max_per_buyer_period`), and the owner has not decided the split bounds (provider 550 to 900, Treasury 50 to 300, burn 0 to 150) that the log enforces. | Price list and purchase records (S163); asp prices (S171) | The `gate` and `max_per_buyer_period` item fields (credits.md CR-11) with the log rule that refuses a purchase that fails them; the owner decision on the bounds recorded in credits.md. | DK94 | The automatic starter grant at sign-up still creates credits with the unsigned mint, because the service must not hold the operator key, so `asp credits audit --require-signed` fails on a live service. | Signed mint by the operator on their own machine (DK67); the unsigned starter path; mintedUnsigned in the audit | A signed route with no service key: the operator signs, ahead of time, a mint into a reserved starter-pool account; a new-tenant claim record, signed by the new agent itself, moves a fixed starter amount from the pool once per sponsor, and the log refuses a claim when the pool is empty or the sponsor already claimed. Alternatively the operator signs batches of starter mints by hand. Schema and vectors in both SDKs, a spec-delta, tests. Done: a sign-up claim on a test service creates no unsigned credit and `asp credits audit --require-signed` passes after it, with the pool never overdrawn. **Closed 2026-11-12 (S173).** The operator funds the reserved starter pool with signed mints on their own machine; an identity signs its own `starter_claim`; the log moves credits out of the pool by fixed rules (100 upfront once per identity, 2 identities per sponsor, 400 more after a qualifying job, never more than the pool holds); the service holds no key and refuses a tenant appending a claim itself. Test `packages/asp-cli/test/starter-signed.test.ts`: a sign-up claim on a test service creates no unsigned credit, the pool is never overdrawn, and `asp credits audit --require-signed` passes. | Split from DK67 2026-11-12 (owner chose operator-machine signing); starter.ts, credits.md 5.1; size M | A | closed |
| DK95 | A database that already holds unsigned mints (the live service before ASP_OPERATOR_DID is set) keeps them: `asp credits audit` shows them as mintedUnsigned, the replay seeds them from a snapshot, and `--require-signed` fails on it. | The unsigned mint counted apart in the audit; signed mints and claims for new credits (DK67, DK94) | Either re-genesis the service (a fresh log where every credit is a signed mint) or a signed adoption record that moves an unsigned amount into the signed total without moving a balance, with the replay rule worked out. Decide with the owner when the beta database is first created. Done: the live service audit passes `--require-signed`. **Progress 2026-11-14 (Worker 1 round 5, S330): the mechanism is built and tested; applying it to the live database is the operator's step, so the row is partial.** Owner decision 2026-11-13 (docs/owner-decisions.md): a signed adoption by the operator, history kept. `asp.mint_adoption/v0.2` (operator-signed, owner-countersigned: `adoption_unauthorised`, `adoption_needs_cosign`, `adoption_exceeds_unsigned`) moves an amount of an account's unsigned minted total into its signed total without moving a balance; the store remembers the adopted amount (`adoptedMinted`, migration `017_mint_adoption.sql`) so the replay seeds the unsigned total as it was before any adoption and `verify()` still passes. Tests in `packages/asp-log/test/economy-slice.test.ts` ("adopting every unsigned credit takes mintedUnsigned to 0 (so --require-signed passes), moves no balance, keeps the minted total, and the replay still verifies"; a partial adoption with later signed mints; the refusals; the store snapshot) and `packages/asp-cli/test/credits-audit.test.ts` (`asp credits adopt` refusals). **Left, so the row stays partial:** Done says the live service audit passes `--require-signed`, which needs the operator and the owner to sign the adoption records on the live database with `asp credits adopt` (owner-checklist); the Postgres column is applied by the service's migration on its next start. | Split from DK67 2026-11-12; build-notes; size M | A | partial |

The over-budget refusals are TRL-5 (`budget_exceeds_treasury`, a budget that would promise more than the Treasury holds less the budgets committed) and TRL-9 (`over_budget`, a payment past its budget), both with tests in `closed-loop.test.ts`. | DK195 | Telemetry's remaining safeguards are not built: the Mandate `telemetry` field (absent means nothing is collected), the refusal of telemetry for a tenant held for a young person (the log has no such tenant flag), local raw storage with redaction of secrets and personal data, retention and `purge --raw` that makes the salted hashes unlinkable, read-only probes that leave the package hash unchanged, and the Python derived-measure library with vectors that give identical numbers to the TypeScript one. | S291 | The Mandate field and its log rule, the tenant flag and `minor_tenant_no_telemetry`, the local store with redaction and purge, the read-only probe test, and the Python library with shared vectors. Done: each is tested as instrumentation.md 13 lists, and the vectors give identical Brier, ECE, surprisal and KL in both languages. **Closed 2026-11-14 (S342).** The five safeguards are built. (1) The Mandate has an optional `telemetry` field `{signals, purposes, retention_days, raw}`: ABSENT MEANS NOTHING IS COLLECTED (the collector's `mayCollect` needs the Mandate to list the signal, as well as a live, agent-countersigned consent); the log refuses a Mandate that lists a signal or purpose no live consent of the agent names (`telemetry_signal_not_consented`, `telemetry_purpose_not_consented`), including after a revocation or expiry. (2) A tenant flagged `minor` (held for a young person, set by the operator) is refused any telemetry consent and any Mandate with a telemetry field with `minor_tenant_no_telemetry` (403) at the service. (3) `LocalRawStore` masks secrets and personal data (emails, phone numbers, account ids) and cuts the text before it is written, keeps a per-record salt with the raw text, expires rows past the consent's retention, and `purgeRaw` removes every text and salt so the salted hash cannot be matched to a candidate text. (4) `runReadOnlyProbe` runs a probe on a throw-away copy of the package, takes only a whole number of permille (no free text) and checks the package digest unchanged. (5) The Python library `asp_core/telemetry.py` gives the same Brier, ECE (withheld under 50 items), calibration bins, surprisal, KL and normative posterior as the TypeScript one, tested against shared vectors `conformance/vectors/telemetry.json` (rounded measures identical, unrounded equal to 1e-9). Tests: `packages/asp-log/test/telemetry-mandate.test.ts` (the field absent and present, no consent, a signal or purpose the consent does not name, revoked and expired consents, and the collector reading no source for any of the nine signals without the field), `packages/asp-cli/test/telemetry-safeguards.test.ts` (the minor tenant at a running service, redaction of a canary secret and fake personal data, retention, purge and unlinkability, the read-only probe with the package hash unchanged), `packages/asp-log/test/telemetry-vectors.test.ts` and `python/tests/test_telemetry.py` (identical numbers in both languages); the existing `telemetry.test.ts` scan still shows no standing code reads telemetry. **Left, split (DK241):** the gateway runtime wiring that reads the Mandate field and feeds the Collector from a real run, `asp telemetry` commands for consent, status and `purge --raw`, the sealed spill to the sponsor's key and the text changes to the privacy notice; those need a live run and the owner. | DK79; docs/institutions/instrumentation.md 7, 13 and 14; size M | A | closed |

| DK294 | The Bank's deposit reward is decided (1 % a month of the deposit, Treasury-paid, capped by a co-signed budget) but deposits, the cap and the reward record are not built, and reward credits must stay inside the closed-loop table. | bank-insurer.md decision 6; closed-loop-coverage.test.ts | Build deposits and the reward with the closed-loop rules: a deposit moves credits from the agent to the Bank's deposit account, a withdrawal returns them, the monthly reward is paid from the Treasury budget up to its cap and never beyond, and the supply audit still balances. Done: tests for deposit, withdrawal, reward within the cap, reward refused beyond the cap and the audit balanced, with the new movements added to the closed-loop index with named tests. **Closed 2026-11-14 (S370).** Bank deposits and the reward are built inside the closed loop. A `deposit_act` has three acts: `deposit` (the holder signs, the Bank countersigns; the credits move to the Bank's deposit account `did:web:asp.local:bank-deposits`, a reserved account no one spends from, and are locked 30 days), `withdraw` (the holder, from day 30; the credits return whole, with no interest from the Bank) and `reward` (the Treasury, for each completed 30 days of a deposit still held: 1 % of it, from the Treasury's own deposit-reward line (a budget for the Treasury with purpose `deposit_reward`) that the owner co-signed, drawn exactly as the newcomer allowance draws its budget, at most 50 credits a month to one holder, never beyond the budget, never in a month the budget does not cover, never on a withdrawn deposit). Closed-loop rows T15 (deposit and withdrawal: a lock and an unlock) and T16 (the reward: a move out of the Treasury within a budget) are in credits.md 5.2 and in the index of `closed-loop-coverage.test.ts`. Tests: `packages/asp-log/test/deposits.test.ts` (deposit, early and exact withdrawal, withdrawal by a stranger and twice; reward not due, exact amount, wrong issuer, wrong budget, within the cap, refused beyond the 5 credit budget as `over_budget`, a 99 credit deposit earning nothing, two deposits over the 50 credit monthly cap refused, a budget for another month closed; a seeded property test of 60 operations in which the credits only move, the deposit account holds exactly the open deposits and the audit stays balanced) and `packages/asp-cli/test/bank-deposits.test.ts` (`asp bank deposit` and `withdraw`). | Owner decision 2026-11-13; DK180, DK71; size M | A | closed |
